,

\displaystyle s_{z_{1}z_{2}}=s_{z_{1}}s_{z_{2}};

•

for any
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, there exists a unique vector
t=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{m})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
such that

\displaystyle s_{z}=a_{1}^{t_{1}}\cdots a_{m}^{t_{m}}.

Then the map
\tau:z\to t
is a group homomorphism;

•

denote by
\mathfrak{a}=(a_{1},\cdots,a_{m})
. For any
t=(t_{1},\cdots,t_{m})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, set

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{t}=a_{1}^{t_{1}}a_{2}^{t_{2}}\cdots a_{m}^{t_{m}};

•

for any
z\in\mathcal{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert w_{z}\rVert\leq C_{\mathcal{Z}}(\lVert\tau(z)\rVert+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)}.

Note.
It is clear that
\log a_{1},\cdots,\log a_{m}
is a set of linearly independent vectors in
\text{Lie}(A)\simeq\mathbb{R}^{\text{rank}(A)}
.

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
3.1

We consider the subgroup
H(\mathcal{S})
(see (
5
) of Section
6.1
) and the restricted representation
\rho|_{H(\mathcal{S})}
. We recall the conditions (
1
) to (
3
) in (
6
) of Section
6.1
. Moreover,
the assumption shows that

\displaystyle\xi_{2}\in\mathcal{H}\quad\text{ and }\quad\Sigma_{l}^{\frac{r_{l}+1}{2}}\cdots\Sigma_{1}^{\frac{r_{1}+1}{2}}\xi_{2}\in\mathcal{H}.

Together with the conditions above, this justifies applying
Theorem
5.1
to conclude that the equation

\displaystyle|U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}\omega=\xi_{2}

has a unique solution
\omega\in\mathcal{H}
with the estimate

(6.2)

\displaystyle\lVert\omega\rVert\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\big\|\Sigma_{l}^{\frac{r_{l}+1}{2}}\cdots\Sigma_{1}^{\frac{r_{1}+1}{2}}\xi_{2}\big\|\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\lVert\xi_{2}\rVert_{S(\mathcal{S}),r_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S})+l},

where
\nu=(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{l})
and
\mathfrak{r}=(r_{1},\cdots,r_{l})
.

Then we have

\displaystyle\big|\langle\rho(a^{n})\xi_{1},\,\xi_{2}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\big\langle\rho(a^{n})\xi_{1},\,|U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}\omega\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{=}\Big|\big\langle|U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}(\rho(a^{n})\xi_{1}),\,\omega\big\rangle\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}\big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}(\rho(a^{n})\xi_{1})\big\|\cdot\lVert\omega\rVert

(6.3)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(3)}}{\leq}C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}(\rho(a^{n})\xi_{1})\big\|\cdot\lVert\xi_{2}\rVert_{S(\mathcal{S}),r_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S})+l}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
1
) of Lemma
6.1
; in
(2)
we use cauchy schwarz inequality; in
(3)
we use (
6.2
).

Further, we have

\displaystyle\big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}(\rho(a^{n})\xi_{1})\big\|

\displaystyle=\Big\|\rho(a^{n})\big(\rho(a^{-n})|U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}\rho(a^{n})\big)\xi_{1}\Big\|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{=}\Big\|\big(\rho(a^{-n})|U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}\rho(a^{n})\big)\xi_{1}\Big\|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{=}(\alpha_{1}(a^{n}))^{-p_{1}}(\alpha_{2}(a^{n}))^{-p_{2}}\cdots(\alpha_{l}(a^{n}))^{-p_{l}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l}}|^{p_{l}}\xi_{1}\Big\|

(6.4)

\displaystyle\leq\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},a^{n})\cdot\big\|\xi_{1}\big\|_{u(\mathcal{S}),r_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S})}

We explain steps.

•

In
(1)
we use the fact that
\rho
is a unitary representation.

•

In
(2)
we note that

\displaystyle\text{Ad}_{(a^{n})^{-1}}U_{\alpha_{i}}=(\alpha_{i}(a^{n}))^{-1}U_{\alpha_{i}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq l.

So that the transformation follows from (
3
) of Lemma
6.1
.

•

In
(3)
we recall notations in (
5
) of Section
6.1
.

It follows from (
6.2
) and (
6.2
) that

\displaystyle\big|\langle\rho(a^{n})\xi_{1},\,\xi_{2}\rangle\big|\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},a^{n})\cdot\big\|\xi_{1}\big\|_{u(\mathcal{S}),r_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S})}\cdot\lVert\xi_{2}\rVert_{S(\mathcal{S}),r_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S})+l}

Then we complete the proof.

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
3.3

6.3.1.
Notations and basic facts

1
.

Since
\mathcal{Z}
is a rank one group, (
9
) of Section
6.1
shows that
\{s_{z}:\,z\in\mathcal{Z}\}
is a rank subgroup of
A
. Then there exists
a\in A
such that
\{s_{z}:\,z\in\mathcal{Z}\}
is generated by
a
. We recall that there exists
w
inside the Weyl group such that

\displaystyle a^{+}=\textbf{w}^{-1}a\textbf{w}\in A^{+}.

Moreover, (
9
) of Section
6.1
shows that for any
g\in\mathcal{Z}
, there exists
t\in\mathbb{R}
such that
s_{g}=a^{t}
. Then we have

(6.5)

\displaystyle\lVert\text{Ad}_{w_{g}}\rVert\leq C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)},\qquad\forall\,g\in\mathcal{Z}.

For any
g_{1},\,g_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}
there exists
t_{1},\,t_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle s_{g_{1}}=a^{t_{1}}\quad\text{and}\quad s_{g_{2}}=a^{t_{2}}.

We note that

\displaystyle(v\textbf{w})^{-1}

\displaystyle g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}(v\textbf{w})=(\textbf{w}^{-1}w_{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}}\textbf{w})(\textbf{w}^{-1}a^{t_{1}-t_{2}}\textbf{w})

(6.6)

\displaystyle=(\textbf{w}^{-1}w_{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}}\textbf{w})(a^{+})^{t_{1}-t_{2}}.

It follows from (
6.5
) that

(6.7)

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{w_{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}}}\big\|\leq C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)}.

2
.

We need the result

Lemma 6.1
.

Suppose
l^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
satisfying
l^{\prime}\leq l
. Define

\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}=\{b=(b_{1},\dots,b_{l^{\prime}})\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{l^{\prime}}\mid b_{i}\in\{0,1\}\text{ for each }i\},

and define

U^{b}=U_{\alpha_{1}}^{b_{1}}\cdots U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}^{b_{l^{\prime}}}.

Then for any
x=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{l^{\prime}})\in[0,1]^{l^{\prime}}
, there exist
2^{l^{\prime}}
constants
0\leq c(b)<1
such that

(1)

\sum_{b\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}c(b)=1
,

(2)

\sum_{b\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}c(b)b=x
,

(3)

for any
\xi\in W^{\infty,u(\mathcal{S})}(\mathcal{H})
, we have

\displaystyle\big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{x_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{x_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}|^{x_{l^{\prime}}}\xi\big\|\leq C_{x}\prod_{b\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}\|U^{b}\xi\|^{c(b)}.

The proof is left for Section
C
.

3
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any
b=(b_{1},\dots,b_{l^{\prime}})\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}
(see Lemma
6.1
) and
a\in A
, define

\displaystyle(\alpha(a))^{b}=\alpha_{1}(a)^{b_{1}}\alpha_{2}(a)^{b_{2}}\cdots\alpha_{l^{\prime}}(a)^{b_{l^{\prime}}}.

Suppose

•

t_{1},\cdots t_{m}\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
with
t=\min_{1\leq i\leq m}t_{i}>0
;

•

\alpha_{i}(a)>1
,
1\leq i\leq l^{\prime}
;

•

and
s_{1},\cdots s_{m}\in\mathbb{G}
with

\displaystyle\lVert\text{Ad}_{s_{i}}\rVert\leq C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{i}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)},\qquad 1\leq i\leq m.

Then for any
0<\varepsilon<1
, any
b=(b_{1},\dots,b_{l^{\prime}})\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}
(see Lemma
6.1
) and any
f_{1},\cdots,f_{m}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X})
, we have

\displaystyle\big\|U^{b}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{m}\pi(a^{t_{i}})(f_{i}\circ s_{i})\big)\big\|_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{Z},\varepsilon,m}\big(\alpha(a^{-t(1-\varepsilon)})\big)^{b}\Pi_{i=1}^{m}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{|b|}}.

The proof is left for Section
D
.

6.3.2.
Proof of (
2
) of Theorem
3.3

We prove by induction on
n
. Since
\mathcal{Z}\subseteq A
, the case of
n=2
follows directly from Theorem
3.1
and (
6.1
) of Section
6.1
.
Assume that the statement holds for
n=k
; that is, for any

g_{1},\dots,g_{k}\in\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{1},\dots,f_{k}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}),

we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ g_{1},\dots,f_{k}\circ g_{k})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{k})\Big|

(6.8)

\displaystyle\leq\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}C_{\epsilon}\,\eta_{\epsilon}\big(\mathcal{S},g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}\big)\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

Suppose

\displaystyle g_{1},\cdots,g_{k+1}\in\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{ and }\quad f_{1},\cdots,f_{k+1}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}).

Since
\mathcal{Z}\subseteq A
, in this case

\displaystyle v=e,\quad\text{and}\quad w_{g}=e,\qquad\forall\,g\in\mathcal{Z}.

Then for each
1\leq i\leq k+1
there exists
t_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
such that

\displaystyle g_{i}=a^{t_{i}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq k+1.

Without loss of generality, assume that

\displaystyle t_{k+1}=\max_{1\leq j\leq k+1}t_{j}.

Then, for each
1\leq i\leq k
, we have

\displaystyle\textbf{w}^{-1}

\displaystyle g_{i}g_{k+1}^{-1}\textbf{w}=\textbf{w}^{-1}a^{t_{i}-t_{k+1}}\textbf{w}=(a^{+})^{t_{i}-t_{k+1}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ a^{t_{1}},f_{2}\circ a^{t_{2}},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ a^{t_{k+1}}\big)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{=}\mathfrak{m}\big(\tilde{f}_{1}\circ(a^{+})^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}},\tilde{f}_{2}\circ(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{k+1}},

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdots,\tilde{f}_{k}\circ(a^{+})^{t_{k}-t_{k+1}},\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2},

(6.9)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdots,\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{k}})\tilde{f}_{k},\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}=f_{i}\circ\textbf{w}^{-1},\quad\tilde{f}_{k+1}=f_{k+1}\circ\textbf{w},\qquad 1\leq i\leq k.

Then we have

(6.10)

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\leq C\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq k+1.

Since
t_{k+1}-t_{i}>0
,
1\leq i\leq k
, choose an index
1\leq i_{0}\leq k
such that

\displaystyle t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}=\min_{1\leq i\leq k}\{t_{k+1}-t_{i}\}>0.

Then, for each
1\leq i\leq k
,

(6.11)

\displaystyle t_{i_{0}}-t_{i}=t_{k+1}-t_{i}-(t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}})>0.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\pi((a^{+})^{t_{i_{0}}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1}\cdot\pi((a^{+})^{t_{i_{0}}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2}\cdots\pi((a^{+})^{t_{i_{0}}-t_{k}})\tilde{f}_{k}.

It follows that

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}})\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big)=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big).

Moreover, by induction assumption (
6.3.2
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k})-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\epsilon}\big(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}|t_{i}-t_{j}|}\big)\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.13)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,1}\eta_{\epsilon}\big(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}|t_{i}-t_{j}|}\big)\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.10
).

Next, Theorem
3.1
shows that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}})\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}})\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.14)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}})\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{k+1}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(1)
we recall notations in (
5
) of Section
6.1
and use again (
6.10
).

Moreover, note that

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k}\lVert\pi((a^{+})^{t_{i_{0}}-t_{i}})\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k}\lVert\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.15)

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(1)
we used the fact that any
v\in\text{Lie}(u(\mathcal{S}))
(recall that
u(\mathcal{S})
is defined in (
5
) of Section
6.1
),

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{(a^{+})^{t}}v\|\leq\lVert v\rVert,\qquad\forall\,t<0.

We also note that

(6.16)

\displaystyle t_{i_{0}}-t_{i}>0,\qquad 1\leq i\leq k.

Then
(1)
follows from (
7
) of Section
6.1
).

It follows from (
6.12
), (
6.3.2
) and (
6.3.2
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\Big|

(6.17)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}})\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

We note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{i})=\mathfrak{m}(f_{i}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq k+1.

This together with (
6.3.2
) and (
6.3.2
) show that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{k+1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}})\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle+\Big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k})\Big||\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{i_{0}}})\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\epsilon}\big(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}|t_{i}-t_{j}|}\big)\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}|\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,1}\,\eta_{\epsilon}\big(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}|t_{i}-t_{j}|}\big)\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

The above estimate shows that the desired bound holds for
n=k+1
.
This completes the induction step and thereby the proof.

Remark 6.3
.

Obtaining (
6.3.2
) relies on the estimates in (
6.10
). However, if
\mathcal{Z}
is not contained in a split Cartan subgroup, then the elements of
\mathcal{Z}
may no longer be semisimple. In other words, the
w_{g}
part may not be trivial. Consequently, the definition of
\tilde{f}_{i}
involves the
w_{g_{i}g_{k+1}^{-1}}
part (see (
6.25
) of Section
6.3.3
), which causes
\lVert\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}
to grow polynomially with respect to
|t_{i}-t_{k+1}|
(see (
6.5
) of Section
6.3.1
). On the other hand, the decay rate in (
6.3.2
) is exponential with respect to
\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}|t_{i}-t_{j}|
. Therefore, if
|t_{i}-t_{k+1}|
becomes larger than

\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}|t_{i}-t_{j}|
, the exponential decay may not overcome the polynomial growth. So, we need a new argument to handle the general case of

\mathcal{Z}
. That is why we need Lemma
6.1
.

6.3.3.
Proof of (
3
) of Theorem
3.3

We prove by induction on
n
.

Base Case
n=2
:
Suppose

\displaystyle g_{1},\,g_{2}\in\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{ and }\quad f_{1},\,f_{2}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}).

By using (
1
) of Section
6.3.1
we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ(g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}),f_{2}\big)\overset{\text{(1)}}{=}\mathfrak{m}\big(\tilde{f}_{1}\circ(a^{+})^{t_{1}-t_{2}},\tilde{f}_{2}\big)

(6.18)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\tilde{f}_{2}\big)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{1}=f_{1}\circ(vw_{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}}\textbf{w}^{-1}),\qquad\tilde{f}_{2}=f_{2}\circ(v\textbf{w}).

From (
7
) of Section
6.1
, we see that

(6.19)

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{2}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\leq C\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

and

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\text{Ad}_{vw_{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}}\textbf{w}^{-1}}\big\|^{r(\mathcal{S})}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.20)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.5
) of Section
6.3.1
.

It follows from Theorem
3.1
that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\tilde{f}_{2}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\lVert\tilde{f}_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|\tilde{f}_{2}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.21)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z},1}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.19
) and (
6.3.3
); in
(2)
we use the fact that
\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})
decays exponentially with respect to
|t_{1}-t_{2}|
.

Since

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{2})=\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\mathfrak{m}(f_{2}).

combining (
6.3.3
) and (
6.3.3
) we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\mathfrak{m}(f_{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z},1}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle=C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z},1}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{1}g_{2}^{-1}})\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

Thus, we proved the case of
n=2
.

Inductive Step:
Suppose the argument holds for
n=k
; that is, for

g_{1},\dots,g_{k}\in\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{1},\dots,f_{k}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}),

we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ g_{1},\dots,f_{k}\circ g_{k})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{k})\Big|

(6.22)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})\,\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Suppose

\displaystyle g_{1},\cdots,g_{k+1}\in\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{ and }\quad f_{1},\cdots,f_{k+1}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}).

There exists
t_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
,
1\leq i\leq k+1
such that

\displaystyle s_{g_{i}}=a^{t_{i}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq k+1.

Without loss of generality, assume

\displaystyle t_{k+1}=\max_{1\leq j\leq k+1}t_{j}.

By using (
1
) of Section
6.3.1
we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ(g_{1}g_{k+1}^{-1}),f_{2}\circ(g_{2}g_{k+1}^{-1}),\cdots,f_{k}\circ(g_{k}g_{k+1}^{-1}),f_{k+1}\big)

(6.23)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{=}\mathfrak{m}\big(\tilde{f}_{1}\circ(a^{+})^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}},\tilde{f}_{2}\circ(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{k+1}},

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdots,\tilde{f}_{k}\circ(a^{+})^{t_{k}-t_{k+1}},\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2},

(6.24)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\cdots,\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{k}})\tilde{f}_{k},\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big)

where

(6.25)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}=f_{i}\circ(vw_{g_{i}g_{k+1}^{-1}}\textbf{w}^{-1}),\quad\tilde{f}_{k+1}=f_{k+1}\circ(v\textbf{w}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq k.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1}\big)\,\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2}\big)\cdots\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{k+1}-t_{k}})\tilde{f}_{k}\big).

Then (
6.24
) shows that

(6.26)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{k+1})=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big).

Next, let

\displaystyle D=\{1\leq i\leq l:\,\alpha_{i}(a^{+})>1\}.

Without loss of generality, write
D=\{1,2,\cdots,l^{\prime}\}
, with
l^{\prime}\leq l
, and set

\displaystyle D(\mathcal{S})=\Pi_{i=1}^{l^{\prime}}H_{\alpha_{i}}

(see (
5
) of Section
6.1
). From (
1
) in (
6
) of Section
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\text{Lie}(D(\mathcal{S}))=\underbrace{\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})\times\cdots\times\mathfrak{sl}(2,\mathbb{R})}_{l^{\prime}}.

We consider the restricted representation of
\big(\pi|_{D(\mathcal{S})},\,L^{2}_{0}(\mathcal{X})\big)
(see (
1
) of Section
6.1
). The assumption shows that
(\pi,\,L^{2}_{0}(\mathcal{X}))
is a unitary representation of
G

with a strong spectral gap without fixed vectors. Then conditions (
2
) and (
3
) in (
6
) of Section
6.1
hold.
Moreover, since
\tilde{f}_{k+1}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}(\mathcal{X})
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{k+1}-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\in L^{2}_{0}(\mathcal{X})\qquad\text{ and }

\displaystyle\Sigma_{l^{\prime}}^{\frac{r_{l^{\prime}}+1}{2}}\cdots\Sigma_{1}^{\frac{r_{1}+1}{2}}\big(\tilde{f}_{k+1}-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\big)\in L^{2}_{0}(\mathcal{X}).

The above discussion justifies applying
Theorem
5.1
to conclude that the equation

\displaystyle|U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}|^{p_{l^{\prime}}}\omega=\tilde{f}_{k+1}-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})

has a unique solution
\omega\in L^{2}_{0}(\mathcal{X})
with the estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\omega\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu(l^{\prime}),\mathfrak{r}(l^{\prime}),\epsilon}\Big\|\Sigma_{l}^{\frac{r_{l^{\prime}}+1}{2}}\cdots\Sigma_{1}^{\frac{r_{l^{\prime}}+1}{2}}\big(\tilde{f}_{k+1}-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\big)\Big\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\lVert\tilde{f}_{k+1}\rVert_{S(\mathcal{S}),r_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S})+l^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\lVert\tilde{f}_{k+1}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.27)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,1}\lVert f_{k+1}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

where
\nu(l^{\prime})=(\nu_{1},\cdots,\nu_{l^{\prime}})
and
\mathfrak{r}(l^{\prime})=(r_{1},\cdots,r_{l^{\prime}})
.

Similar to (
6.2
) in Section
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{k+1}-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}|^{p_{l^{\prime}}}\mathcal{F}\big\|\cdot\lVert\tilde{f}_{k+1}\rVert_{S(\mathcal{S}),r_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S})+l^{\prime}}

(6.28)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon}\big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}|^{p_{l^{\prime}}}\mathcal{F}\big\|\cdot\lVert f_{k+1}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Let

\displaystyle t=\min_{1\leq i\leq k}\big|t_{k+1}-t_{i}\big|.

By assumption
t>0
. We also recall that
\alpha_{i}(a^{+})>1
,
1\leq i\leq l^{\prime}
.
This allows us to apply Lemma
6.1
to conclude that

\displaystyle\big\||U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{p_{1}}|U_{\alpha_{2}}|^{p_{2}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}|^{p_{l^{\prime}}}\mathcal{F}\big\|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}}}\prod_{\textsf{d}\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}\big\|\mathcal{T}^{\textsf{d}}\mathcal{F}\big\|^{c(\textsf{d})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{=}C_{p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}}}\prod_{\textsf{d}\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}\big\|U_{\alpha_{1}}^{d_{1}}\cdots U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}^{d_{l^{\prime}}}\mathcal{F}\big\|^{c(\textsf{d})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(3)}}{\leq}C_{p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}}}\prod_{\textsf{d}\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}\Big(C_{\mathcal{Z},\epsilon,k}\big(\alpha((a^{+})^{-t(1-\varepsilon)})\big)^{\textsf{d}}\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{|\textsf{d}|}}\Big)^{c(\textsf{d})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\prod_{\textsf{d}\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}\big(\alpha((a^{+})^{-t(1-\varepsilon)}\big)^{\textsf{d}c(\textsf{d})}(\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{l^{\prime}}})^{c(\textsf{d})}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(4)}}{=}C_{p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\big(\alpha((a^{+})^{-t(1-\varepsilon)})\big)^{(p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}})}\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{l^{\prime}}}

(6.29)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(5)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{l}}.

We explain the steps.

•

In
(1)
we use the notation

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}^{\textsf{d}}=|U_{\alpha_{1}}|^{d_{1}}\cdots|U_{\alpha_{l^{\prime}}}|^{d_{l^{\prime}}},\qquad\text{for }\textsf{d}=(d_{1},\cdots,d_{l^{\prime}});

and we note that

(6.30)

\displaystyle\sum_{\textsf{d}\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}c(\textsf{d})\textsf{d}=(p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}})\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{\textsf{d}\in\mathbb{L}_{l^{\prime}}}c(\textsf{d})=1.

•

In
(2)
we use (
2
) of Lemma
6.1
.

•

In
(3)
we note that

\displaystyle t_{k+1}-t_{i}>0,\qquad 1\leq i\leq k

and

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{vw_{g_{i}g_{k+1}^{-1}}}\big\|\leq\|\text{Ad}_{v}\big\|\,\|\text{Ad}_{w_{g_{i}g_{k+1}^{-1}}}\leq\big\|C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{i}-t_{k+1}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)}

(see (
6.7
) of Section
6.3.1
).
Then the estimate follows from Lemma
6.2
, where we choose
\varepsilon
sufficiently small such that

(6.31)

\displaystyle(1-\varepsilon)(\frac{1-\nu_{i}}{2}-\frac{3\epsilon}{4})>\frac{1-\nu_{i}}{2}-\epsilon,\qquad 1\leq i\leq l.

•

In
(4)
we use (
6.30
).

•

In
(5)
from (
6.31
) we have

\displaystyle\big(\alpha((a^{+})^{-t(1-\varepsilon)})\big)^{(p_{1},\cdots,p_{l^{\prime}})}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{i=1}^{l^{\prime}}\alpha_{i}((a^{+})^{-t(1-\varepsilon)})^{\frac{1-\nu_{i}}{2}-\frac{3\epsilon}{4}}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\alpha_{i}((a^{+})^{-t(1-\varepsilon)})^{\frac{1-\nu_{i}}{2}-\frac{3\epsilon}{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\alpha_{i}((a^{+})^{t})^{\frac{-1+\nu_{i}}{2}+\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t})

It now follows from (
6.3.3
) and (
6.3.3
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{k+1}-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{l}}\cdot\lVert f_{k+1}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

By using (
6.3.3
) we also have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ(g_{1}g_{k+1}^{-1}),f_{2}\circ(g_{2}g_{k+1}^{-1}),\cdots,f_{k}\circ(g_{k}g_{k+1}^{-1})\big)\big)\Big|\,|\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{0}})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\cdot\lVert f_{k+1}\rVert_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{0}})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(1)
we use the inductive assumption (
6.3.3
) and let

\displaystyle t_{0}=\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}\big|(t_{k+1}-t_{i})-(t_{k+1}-t_{j})\big|=\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}\big|t_{i}-t_{j}\big|.

By using (
6.26
) and combining the estimates above, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{k+1}-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\big)\Big|+\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\mathcal{F},\,\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{k+1})\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle+C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{0}})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\nu,\mathfrak{r},\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z},1}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}|t_{i}-t_{j}|})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle=\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}|t_{i}-t_{j}|C_{\epsilon,k,\mathcal{Z},1}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})\cdot\Pi_{i=1}^{k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

This completes the inductive step.

Hence, by induction the claim (
3
) is proved.

6.3.4.
Proof of (
1
) of Theorem
3.3

Suppose

\displaystyle g_{1},\,g_{2},\,g_{3}\in\mathcal{Z},\qquad f_{1},\,f_{2},\,f_{3}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})=0,1\leq i\leq 3.

There exists
t_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
,
1\leq i\leq 3
such that

\displaystyle s_{g_{i}}=a^{t_{i}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq 3.

Without loss of generality, assume that
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{3}
. Then

\displaystyle t_{3}-t_{1}=\max_{1\leq i\neq j\leq 3}|t_{i}-t_{j}|

Case I
: Suppose
t_{3}-t_{2}>\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})
.

Similar to (
6.24
) we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},f_{3}\circ g_{3}\big)

(6.32)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{3}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\pi((a^{+})^{t_{3}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2},\tilde{f}_{3}\big)

where

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}=f_{i}\circ(vw_{g_{i}g_{3}^{-1}}\textbf{w}^{-1}),\quad\tilde{f}_{3}=f_{3}\circ(v\textbf{w}),\qquad i=1,\,2.

By arguments analogous to (
6.19
) and (
6.3.3
), we have

(6.33)

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{3}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\leq C\lVert f_{3}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

and, for
i=1,\,2
,

(6.34)

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\leq C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{i}-t_{3}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}.

Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1}\cdot\tilde{f}_{2}.

It follows that

(6.35)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{3}-t_{2}})\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{3}\big)=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},f_{3}\circ g_{3}\big).

We note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{3})=\mathfrak{m}(f_{3})=0.

Then Theorem
3.1
shows that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{3}-t_{2}})\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{3}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{3}-t_{2}})\mathcal{F},\,\tilde{f}_{3}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{3})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{3}-t_{2}})\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|\tilde{f}_{3}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,1}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{3}-t_{2}})\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{3}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.36)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,1}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})})\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{3}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\,.

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.33
); in
(2)
uses the assumption
t_{3}-t_{2}>\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})
.

Next, note that

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\big\|\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1}\big\|_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|\tilde{f}_{2}\big\|_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C\,\lVert\tilde{f}_{1}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\lVert\tilde{f}_{2}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.37)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{1}-t_{3}|+1)^{2\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}.

We explain steps.

•

In
(1)
we used the fact that any
v\in\text{Lie}(u(\mathcal{S}))
(recall that
u(\mathcal{S})
is defined in (
5
) of Section
6.1
),

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{(a^{+})^{t}}v\|\leq\lVert v\rVert,\qquad\forall\,t<0.

We also note that
t_{2}-t_{1}>0
. Then
(1)
follows from (
7
) of Section
6.1
).

•

In
(2)
we use (
6.34
) and note that
|t_{1}-t_{3}|\geq|t_{2}-t_{3}|
.

It follows from (
6.35
), (
6.3.4
) and (
6.3.4
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},f_{3}\circ g_{3}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})})(|t_{1}-t_{3}|+1)^{2\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}

\displaystyle\cdot\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|f_{3}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z},1}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})})\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Hence, we get the result.

Case II
: Suppose
t_{3}-t_{2}<\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})
. Then

\displaystyle t_{2}-t_{1}

\displaystyle=t_{2}-t_{3}-(t_{1}-t_{3})>\frac{1}{2}(t_{1}-t_{3})-(t_{1}-t_{3})

(6.38)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})>0.

Similarly as before, we rewrite

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},f_{3}\circ g_{3}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{3}\circ g_{3},f_{2}\circ g_{2}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{3}})\tilde{f}_{3},\tilde{f}_{2}\big)

where

(6.39)

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}=f_{i}\circ(vw_{g_{i}g_{2}^{-1}}\textbf{w}^{-1}),\quad\tilde{f}_{2}=f_{2}\circ(v\textbf{w}),\qquad i=1,\,3.

The estimates

(6.40)

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{2}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\leq C\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

and for
i=1,\,3

(6.41)

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\leq C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{i}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}

hold as before. Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{3}})\tilde{f}_{3}\cdot\tilde{f}_{2}.

It follows that

(6.42)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\,\mathcal{G}\big)=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},f_{3}\circ g_{3}\big).

We note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{1})=\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})=0.

Then Theorem
3.1
shows that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\,\mathcal{G}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\,\mathcal{G}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\tilde{f}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{G})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\lVert\tilde{f}_{1}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|\mathcal{G}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})(|t_{3}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}

(6.43)

\displaystyle\cdot\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|\mathcal{G}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.41
).

Next, we estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{G}\rVert_{S(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C\,\big\|\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{3}})\tilde{f}_{3}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\,\big\|\tilde{f}_{2}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C\,\lVert\tilde{f}_{3}\rVert_{S(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}\lVert\tilde{f}_{2}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.44)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}C_{\mathcal{Z}}(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}\lVert f_{3}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

We explain steps.

•

In
(1)
we used the fact that any
v\in\text{Lie}(S(\mathcal{S}))
(recall that
S(\mathcal{S})
is defined in (
5
) of Section
6.1
),

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{(a^{+})^{t}}v\|\leq\lVert v\rVert,\qquad\forall\,t>0.

We also note that
t_{2}-t_{3}<0
. Then
(1)
follows from (
7
) of Section
6.1
).

•

In
(2)
we use (
6.34
) and note that
|t_{1}-t_{3}|\geq|t_{2}-t_{3}|
.

It follows from (
6.42
), (
6.3.4
) and (
6.3.4
) that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},f_{3}\circ g_{3}\big)

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi((a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\,\mathcal{G}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})(|t_{3}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}

\displaystyle\cdot(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}\cdot\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\lVert f_{3}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})(|t_{3}-t_{2}|+1)^{2\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S})}

\displaystyle\cdot\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\,\lVert f_{3}\rVert_{C^{r(\mathcal{S})}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,\mathcal{Z}}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{\frac{1}{2}(t_{3}-t_{1})})\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.3.4
) and the fact that
\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},(a^{+})^{t_{2}-t_{1}})
decays exponentially with respect to
|t_{1}-t_{2}|
.

Hence, we complete the proof.

6.4.
Proof of (
1
) of Theorem
3.4

We prove by induction on
n
. The case of
n=2
follows from Theorem
3.1
directly.

Suppose the argument holds for
n=k
; that is, for

g_{1},\dots,g_{k}\in\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{1},\dots,f_{k}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}),

we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ g_{1},\dots,f_{k}\circ g_{k})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{k})\Big|

(6.45)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{(k-1)|\mathcal{S}|}}\,\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Inductive Step:
Suppose

\displaystyle g_{1},\cdots,g_{k+1}\in\mathcal{Z}\quad\text{ and }\quad f_{1},\cdots,f_{k+1}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}).

There exists
t_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
1\leq i\leq k+1
such that

\displaystyle s_{g_{i}}=\mathfrak{a}^{t_{i}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq k+1.

Without loss of generality, assume

\displaystyle\lVert t_{1}-t_{k+1}\rVert=\max_{1\leq i,j\leq k+1}\lVert t_{i}-t_{j}\rVert.

Step 1: Partitioning the Set
\{1,2,\cdots,k\}

There exists
1\leq i_{0}\leq l
such that

\displaystyle\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}})^{\frac{1}{l}}\geq\min\{\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{1+k}})^{\frac{-1+\nu_{i}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}},\,\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{1}})^{\frac{-1+\nu_{i}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\}.

Without loss of generality, assume that

(6.46)

\displaystyle\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}})^{\frac{1}{l}}\geq\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{1}})^{\frac{-1+\nu_{i}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}.

Set

(6.47)

\displaystyle D_{1}

\displaystyle=\{1\leq i\leq k:\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})>1\}\qquad\text{and}

(6.48)

\displaystyle D_{2}

\displaystyle=\{1\leq i\leq k:\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})\leq 1\}.

Then
D_{1}
and
D_{2}
form a partition of the set
\{1,\dots,k\}
. It is clear that
1\in D_{1}
and
|D_{1}|\leq k
.

Next, set

(6.49)

\displaystyle c=\max_{i\in D_{1}}\{\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})\}\geq\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+1}-t_{1}})>1.

Then the
k+1
constants
c^{\frac{j}{k}}
,
0\leq j\leq k
divide the interval
[1,c]
into
k
subintervals
[c^{\frac{j}{k}},c^{\frac{j+1}{k}}]
,
0\leq j\leq k-1
.
All values

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}}),\qquad i\in D_{1},

except those equal to
c
, lie in
(1,c)
. Since there are at most
|D_{1}|-1\leq k-1
such values, there exists some
0\leq j\leq k-1
, such that none of these numbers fall inside
the interval
(c^{\frac{j}{k}},c^{\frac{j+1}{k}})
. Consequently, for all
i\in D_{1}
, we have either

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})-c^{\frac{j}{k}}\leq 0\quad\text{or}\quad\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})-c^{\frac{j+1}{k}}\geq 0.

Define the subcollections

\displaystyle D_{1,1}

\displaystyle=\{i\in D_{1}:\text{ if }\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})-c^{\frac{j}{k}}\leq 0\}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle D_{1,2}

\displaystyle=\{i\in D_{1}:\text{ if }\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})-c^{\frac{j+1}{k}}\geq 0\}.

Note
.
D_{1,1}
and
D_{1,2}
form a partition of
D_{1}
and
1\in D_{1,2}
.

Choose indices
\mathfrak{q}_{1}\in D_{1,1}
and
\mathfrak{q}_{2}\in D_{1,2}
such that

(6.50)

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}})

\displaystyle=\max_{i\in D_{1,1}}\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})\leq c^{\frac{j}{k}},\qquad\text{and}

(6.51)

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\min_{i\in D_{1,2}}\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{i}})\geq c^{\frac{j+1}{k}}.

Then

(6.52)

\displaystyle D_{1,1},\,\,D_{1,2}\,\,\text{and}\,\,D_{2}\text{ form a partition of the set }\{1,\dots,k\}.

Step 2: Construction of a strongly orthogonal system

Let
\mathcal{S}_{1}=\{\alpha_{i_{0}}\}
. Then:

(1)

\mathcal{S}_{1}
is a strongly orthogonal system of
A
(recall (
4
) of Section
3.1
).

(2)

\text{Lie}(u(\mathcal{S}_{1}))=U_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}
and
\text{Lie}(S(\mathcal{S}_{1}))
is spanned by
U_{\alpha_{i_{0}}^{-1}}
and
X_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}
(recall (
5
) of Section
3.1
).

(3)

We have

\displaystyle r(\mathcal{S}_{1})=\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})=\lfloor\frac{1+\nu_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}}{2}\rfloor+1

\displaystyle\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S}_{1},a)=\alpha_{i_{0}}(a^{+})^{\frac{-1+\nu_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}}{2}+\epsilon},\qquad\forall\,a\in A.

(recall (
7
) of Section
3.1
).

Step 3: Construction of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}

We recall that (see (
9
) of Section
6.1
)

\displaystyle v^{-1}

\displaystyle g_{i}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1}v=w_{g_{i}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1}}\mathfrak{a}^{t_{i}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq k+1.

Then we rewrite

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ(g_{1}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1}),f_{2}\circ(g_{2}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1}),\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ(g_{k+1}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1})\big)

(6.53)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1},\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2},\cdots,\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{k+1}})\tilde{f}_{k+1}\big),

where

\displaystyle\tilde{f}_{i}=f_{i}\circ(vw_{g_{i}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1}}),\qquad 1\leq i\leq k+1.

We recall (
6.52
) and then group the functions, rewriting (
6.53
) as

(6.54)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},f_{2}\circ g_{2},\cdots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1}\big)=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\mathcal{F}_{1},\,\mathcal{F}_{2}\big)

where

(6.55)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{i}})\tilde{f}_{i},\qquad\text{and}

(6.56)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{2}

\displaystyle=\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\,\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i}})\tilde{f}_{i}.

By arguments analogous to (
6.3.3
), we have

\displaystyle\lVert\tilde{f}_{i}\rVert_{C^{j}}

\displaystyle\leq\big\|\text{Ad}_{vw_{g_{1}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1}}}\big\|^{j}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{j}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{\mathcal{Z},j}(\|t_{1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}\|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)j}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{j}}

(6.57)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}C_{\mathcal{Z},j}(\|t_{1}-t_{1+k}\|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)j}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{j}}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
9
) of Section
6.1
; in
(2)
we note that

\displaystyle\|t_{1}-t_{k+1}\|=\max_{1\leq i,j\leq k+1}\lVert t_{i}-t_{j}\rVert\geq\|t_{1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}\|.

Step 4: Norm Estimates of
\mathcal{F}_{1}
and
\mathcal{F}_{2}

We have

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}_{1}\rVert

{}_{u(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{r(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}\leq C_{k}\,\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\big\|\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{i}})\tilde{f}_{i}\big\|_{u(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{r(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}

\displaystyle\overset{(x)}{\leq}C_{k}\,\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\big\|\tilde{f}_{i}\big\|_{u(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{r(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}

(6.58)

\displaystyle\overset{(y)}{\leq}C_{\mathcal{Z},k}\,\prod_{i\in D_{1,2}}\big\{(\|t_{1}-t_{1+k}\|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)r(\mathcal{S}_{1})}\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{u(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{r(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}\big\}

and

\displaystyle\lVert\mathcal{F}_{2}\rVert

{}_{S(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}\leq C_{k}\,\prod_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\big\|\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i}})\tilde{f}_{i}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}

\displaystyle\overset{(y_{1})}{\leq}C_{k}\,\prod_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\big\|\tilde{f}_{i}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}

\displaystyle\overset{(y)}{\leq}C_{\mathcal{Z},k}\prod_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\big\{(\|t_{1}-t_{1+k}\|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}

(6.59)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}\big\}.

We explain steps.

•

In
(x)
for any
i\in D_{1,2}
we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}})})\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}\big(\min_{r\in D_{1,2}}\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{r}})\big)^{-1}\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{k+1}-t_{i})})\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle=1.

Here in
(*)
we use (
6.51
). Then for
U_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}
, which spans
\text{Lie}(u(\mathcal{S}_{1}))
(see (
2
)), we have

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{i})}}U_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}\|=\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{i})})\leq 1.

Then
(x)
follows from (
7
) of Section
6.1
).

•

In
(y)
we use (
6.4
).

•

in
(y_{1})
for any
i\in D_{1,1}
we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}})})\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}\big(\max_{r\in D_{1,1}}\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{r}})\big)^{-1}\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{k+1}-t_{i})})\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle=1.

Here in
(*)
we use (
6.50
).

For any
i\in D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}
, we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}})})\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{i}})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}})})

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(2)}}{\leq}1.

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.48
); in
(2)
we recall that
\mathfrak{q}_{1}\in D_{1,1}\subseteq D_{1}
and then use (
6.47
).

This shows that for any
i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}
we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i}})\leq 1.

Then for
U_{\alpha_{i_{0}}^{-1}}
and
X_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}
, which spans
\text{Lie}(S(\mathcal{S}_{1}))
(see (
2
)), we have

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i})}}U_{\alpha_{i_{0}}^{-1}}\|

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i}})\leq 1\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\|\text{Ad}_{\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{i})}}X_{\alpha_{i_{0}}}\|

\displaystyle=1.

Then
(y_{2})
follows from (
7
) of Section
6.1
).

Step 5: We show that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\mathcal{F}_{1},\,\mathcal{F}_{2}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\Big|

(6.60)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{Z},k,\epsilon}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}.

By (
1
) of Step 2, from Theorem
3.1
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\mathcal{F}_{1},\,\mathcal{F}_{2}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S}_{1},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\lVert\mathcal{F}_{1}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{r(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}\,\big\|\mathcal{F}_{2}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}

(6.61)

\displaystyle\overset{(x)}{\leq}C_{\epsilon}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\lVert\mathcal{F}_{1}\rVert_{u(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{r(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}\,\big\|\mathcal{F}_{2}\big\|_{S(\mathcal{S}_{1}),C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}.

Here in
(x)
we note that

(6.62)

\displaystyle\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})=\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{-(t_{k+1}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}})})\overset{\text{(*)}}{\geq}c^{\frac{j+1}{k}}c^{-\frac{j}{k}}=c^{\frac{1}{k}}.

Here in
(*)
we use (
6.50
) and (
6.51
).

Recalling (
3
) and noteing that
c>1
(see (
6.49
)), then (
6.62
) implies that

\displaystyle\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S}_{1},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})

\displaystyle=\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})^{\frac{-1+\nu_{i}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\leq(c^{\frac{1}{k}})^{\frac{-1+\nu_{i}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(*)}}{\leq}\big(\alpha_{i_{0}}(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1+k}-t_{1}})^{\frac{1}{k}}\big)^{\frac{-1+\nu_{i}}{2}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}}\overset{(**)}{\leq}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}

Here in
(*)
we use (
6.49
)); in

we use (
6.46
).

It follows from (
6.4
), (
6.4
) and (
6.4
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\mathcal{F}_{1},\,\mathcal{F}_{2}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{Z},k,\epsilon}\eta_{\frac{\epsilon}{2}}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}(\|t_{1}-t_{1+k}\|+1)^{\dim(G\ltimes_{\iota}V)\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})(k+1)}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{Z},k,\epsilon}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{k+1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}

(6.63)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{Z},k,\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}.

Step 6: We show that

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})-\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|

(6.64)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

From (
6.53
) and (
6.54
), we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\mathcal{F}_{1}\big)=\mathfrak{m}\big(\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}f_{i}\circ(g_{i}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1})\big)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}f_{i}\circ g_{i}\big)

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}f_{i}\circ(g_{i}g_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}^{-1})\big)

(6.65)

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{m}\big(\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}f_{i}\circ g_{i}\big).

By inductive assumption (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})-\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{|D_{1,2}-1|,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{i\neq j\in D_{1,2}}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{(|D_{1,2}-1)|\mathcal{S}|}}\,\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.66)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

and

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})-\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{|d-1|,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{i\neq j\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{(d-1)|\mathcal{S}|}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

(6.67)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

where
d=|D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}|
.

Consequently, from (
6.4
) and (
6.4
) we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})-\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|\,|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\lVert f_{i}\circ g_{i}\rVert_{C^{0}}

\displaystyle\overset{(x)}{\leq}C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Here in
(x)
we recall (
6.52
).

From (
6.4
) we have

\displaystyle\big|\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|\,\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})-\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{0}}\cdot C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Combining the above estimates, we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})-\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|

\displaystyle\leq\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})-\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|\,|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})|

\displaystyle+\big|\Pi_{i\in D_{1,2}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|\,\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})-\Pi_{i\in D_{1,1}\cup D_{2}\cup\{k+1\}}\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{lk}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq k+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

Then we get the result.

Step 7: We show that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ g_{1},\dots,f_{k+1}\circ g_{k+1})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{k+1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{k,\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq k+1}\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},s_{g_{i}g_{j}^{-1}})^{\frac{1}{k|\mathcal{S}|}}\,\Pi_{i=1}^{k}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S})}}.

In fact, the estimate is a direct consequence of (
6.54
), (
6.4
) and (
6.4
).

This completes the inductive step. Hence, by induction the claim (
1
) is proved.

6.5.
Proof of (
2
) of Theorem
3.4

Suppose

\displaystyle g_{1},\,g_{2},\,g_{3}\in\mathcal{Z},\qquad f_{1},\,f_{2},\,f_{3}\in C_{c}^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})=0,1\leq i\leq 3.

We assume notations in Section
6.4
. There exists
t_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
1\leq i\leq 3
such that

\displaystyle s_{g_{i}}=\mathfrak{a}^{t_{i}},\qquad 1\leq i\leq 3.

Without loss of generality, assume that
t_{1}\leq t_{2}\leq t_{3}
. Then

\displaystyle\lVert t_{3}-t_{1}\rVert=\max_{1\leq i\neq j\leq 3}\|t_{i}-t_{j}\|

We recall how we group
f_{1},\,f_{2},\,f_{3}
to define
\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})
and
\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})
(see (
6.55
) and (
6.56
)). We recall that
1\in D_{1,2}
(see
Note
). This implies that either

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}=\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}_{2}=\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2}\cdot\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{3}})\tilde{f}_{3}

or

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{1}=\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{1}})\tilde{f}_{1}\cdot\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}-t_{2}})\tilde{f}_{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{F}_{2}=\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{3}})\tilde{f}_{3}.

For the former, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})=\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})=0.

For the latter, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})=\mathfrak{m}(f_{3})=0.

In either case, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{1})\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}_{2})=0.

Thus, it follows from (
6.54
) and (
6.4
) that

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ g_{1},

\displaystyle f_{2}\circ g_{2},f_{3}\circ g_{3}\big)\Big|=\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(\pi(\mathfrak{a}^{t_{\mathfrak{q}_{1}}-t_{\mathfrak{q}_{2}}})\mathcal{F}_{1},\,\mathcal{F}_{2}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\mathcal{Z},\epsilon}\,\eta_{\epsilon}(\mathcal{S},\mathfrak{a}^{t_{1}-t_{3}})^{\frac{1}{2l}}\,\Pi_{1\leq i\leq 3}\big\|f_{i}\big\|_{C^{\bar{r}(\mathcal{S}_{1})}}.

Hence, we complete the proof.

7.
Study of codicological equations, nilpotent case I

7.1.
Basic notations

We recall notations in Section
4.3
. Suppose
E\subset\mathfrak{n}_{k}
is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace. Let
\exp(E)
denote the connected subgroup with
Lie algebra
E
. Then
\exp(E)\cap\Gamma
is a lattice of
\exp(E)
. Set

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{E}=\exp(E)/\exp(E)\cap\Gamma.

Then
\mathbb{T}_{E}
is a
\dim E
-dimensional torus.

1
.

We note that
\pi\big(\exp(E)\cap\Gamma\big)
acts trivially on
L^{2}(\mathcal{X},\varrho)
. Therefore, the restriction

\pi|_{\exp(E)}
naturally induces a unitary representation of
\mathbb{T}_{E}
on
L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
; we continue to denote this representation by

\pi|_{\mathbb{T}_{E}}
. In particular, for any
f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
we have

\displaystyle(\pi(t)f)(x)=f([t^{-1}]x),\qquad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{T}_{E}.

where
[t^{-1}]
denotes the equivalence
class of
t^{-1}
in
\exp(E)
.

Let
\varrho_{E}
be the
\exp(E)
-invariant
probability measure on
\mathbb{T}_{E}
. We say that a function
f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
is a
\mathbb{T}_{E}
-
fixed
if

\displaystyle\pi(t)f=f,\qquad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{T}_{E}.

For any
f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
set

\displaystyle f_{E,o}=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{E}}\pi(t)fd\varrho_{E}(t)\quad\text{and}\quad f_{E,\bot}=f-f_{E,o}.

Finally, we define

\displaystyle L_{E,o}=\{f_{E,o}:f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\}\quad\text{and}\quad L_{E,\bot}=\{f-f_{E,o}:f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})\}.

It is clear that
f_{E,o}
is
\mathbb{T}_{E}
-fixed and

(7.1)

\displaystyle f_{E,o}(x)=f_{E,o}(vx),\qquad\forall\,v\in\exp(E).

This implies that if
f_{1},\,f_{2}\in L_{E,o}
, then

(7.2)

\displaystyle f_{1}\cdot f_{2}\in L_{E,o}.

2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an automorphism of
\mathcal{X}
. Moreover, suppose
E
is
d\mathcal{A}
-invariant. Then
\mathcal{X}=N/\Gamma
is a nilmanifold that can be viewed as a torus bundle

\mathbb{T}_{E}=\exp(E)/(\exp(E)\cap\Gamma)

over the quotient nilmanifold
\mathcal{X}_{E}
, where

\mathcal{X}_{E}:=(N/\exp(E))\bigl/\bigl(\Gamma/(\exp(E)\cap\Gamma)\bigr).

Since
E
is
d\mathcal{A}
-invariant,
\exp(E)
is
\mathcal{A}
-invariant.
Therefore,
\mathcal{A}
induces a natural automorphism on
N/\exp(E)
, which we denote by
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{X}_{E}}
.

For any
f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
,
f_{E,o}
can be viewed as a function on
\mathcal{X}_{E}
, as
f_{E,o}
is defined by averaging along the torus bundle
\mathbb{T}_{E}
.
Moreover, there exist a unique
N/\exp(E)
-invariant
probability measure
\varrho_{1,E}
on
\mathcal{X}_{E}
such that

(7.3)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}fd\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{E}}f_{E,o}d\varrho_{1,E},\qquad\forall\,f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X}).

7.2.
Cohomological equation over Diophantine subspace

Suppose
E\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k}
is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace. The purpose of this part it to prove the following result:

Theorem 7.1
.

Suppose
V\subset E
is a Diophantine subspace (see Section
4.3.2
) and
V_{1}
is a subspace of
E
such that

\displaystyle E=V\oplus V_{1}.

Fix a basis
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}\}
of
V
. For any
f\in W^{\infty,\exp(E)}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))
we can write

\displaystyle f_{E,\bot}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V}f_{i},

where
f_{i}\in L_{E,\bot}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim V
, such that the following are satisfied:

(1)

for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
we have

\displaystyle\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\leq C\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(E),r},\qquad\forall\,r\geq 0;

(2)

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
, the equation

\displaystyle v_{i}^{n}\varphi_{i,n}=f_{i}

has a solution
\varphi_{i,n}\in L_{E,\bot}
with the estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{i,n}\rVert\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n}\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),n\dim E},

where
\exp(V_{1})
is the subgroup of
N
with Lie algebra
V_{1}
.

Proof.

Step 1: Decomposition of
(\pi|_{\mathbb{T}_{E}},\,L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))

We recall the discussion in Section
5.1
. Since the unitary dual of
\mathbb{T}_{E}
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}
via the characters:

z\to e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot t}
,
t\in\mathbb{T}_{E}
, the unitary representation
(\pi|_{\mathbb{T}_{E}},\,L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))
has a decomposition:

(7.4)

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathcal{X})=\bigoplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\mathcal{H}_{z},

such that for any
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
, we can write

\displaystyle\xi=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\xi_{z}

where
\xi_{z}\in\mathcal{H}_{z}
for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}
; and

(7.5)

\displaystyle\pi(t)\xi=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot t}\xi_{z},\qquad\forall\,t\in\mathbb{T}_{E}.

Moreover, for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

(7.6)

\displaystyle(\Delta_{E})^{n}\xi=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\big(1+(4\pi^{2}\lVert z\rVert^{2})^{n}\big)\xi_{z},

(
\Delta_{E}
is defined in (
4.5
) of Section
4.3.2
) where

\displaystyle\lVert z\rVert^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim E}z_{i}^{2},\qquad\forall\,z=(z_{1},\cdots,z_{\dim E})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}.

It is clear that for any
\xi\in W^{r,\,\exp(E)}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))
,
r\geq 0
, we have

(7.7)

\displaystyle C_{r}^{-1}\lVert(\Delta_{E})^{\frac{r}{2}}\xi\rVert\leq\lVert\xi\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\leq\lVert(\Delta_{E})^{\frac{r}{2}}\xi\rVert.

The decomposition (
7.4
) induces a decomposition of the subspace of the Sobolev spaces (see Section
5.1
): for each
r\in\mathbb{R}
, there is an orthogonal splitting

(7.8)

\displaystyle W^{r,\,\exp(E)}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))=\bigoplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}W^{r,\,\exp(E)}(\mathcal{H}_{z}).

Step 2: Identification of
\xi_{E,o}
and
\xi_{E,\bot}

We show that for any
\xi\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})

(7.9)

\displaystyle\xi_{E,o}=\xi_{0}\quad\text{and}\quad\xi_{E,\bot}=\oplus_{0\neq z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\xi_{z}.

In fact, we have

\displaystyle\xi_{E,o}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{E}}\pi(t)\xi d\varrho_{E}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}_{E}}\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot t}\xi_{z}d\varrho_{E}(t)

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{=}\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\big(\int_{\mathbb{T}_{E}}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot t}d\varrho_{E}(t)\big)\xi_{z}

\displaystyle=\xi_{0}.

Here in
(1)
we note that the integral vanishes unless
z=0
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\xi_{E,\bot}=\oplus_{0\neq z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\xi_{z}.

It follows immediately from the decomposition (
7.8
) that for any
r\geq 0
and any
\xi\in W^{r,\,\exp(E)}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))

(7.10)

\displaystyle\lVert\xi_{E,\bot}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\leq\lVert\xi\rVert_{\exp(E),r}.

Then we get the result.

Step 3: Decomposition of
f_{E,\bot}

We define two formal vectors

\displaystyle f_{[i]}=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}(f_{[i]})_{z},\qquad j=1,\,2,

by setting, for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}

(f_{[1]})_{z}=\begin{cases}(f_{E,\bot})_{z},&\text{if }\sum_{j=1}^{\dim V}|z\cdot v_{j}|\geq 1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

and

(f_{[2]})_{z}=\begin{cases}(f_{E,\bot})_{z},&\text{if }\sum_{j=1}^{\dim V}|z\cdot v_{j}|<1,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

From the decomposition (
7.8
), for any
r\geq 0
we have

(7.11)

\displaystyle\lVert f_{[i]}\rVert_{S,r}\leq\lVert f_{E,\bot}\rVert_{S,r}\overset{(x)}{\leq}\lVert f\rVert_{S,r},\qquad i=1,\,2

for any subgroup
S\subseteq\exp(E)
. Here in
(x)
we use (
7.10
).

Moreover, for any
r\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\big(\lVert f_{[2]}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\big(\lVert(f_{[2]})_{z}\rVert_{\exp(E),r,\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(x)}{\leq}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}C_{r,V_{1}}\big(\lVert(f_{[2]})_{z}\rVert_{\exp(V),r,\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}C_{r,V_{1}}\big(\lVert(f_{[2]})_{z}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r,\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(y)}{\leq}\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}C_{r,V_{1},1}\big(\lVert(f_{[2]})_{z}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}C_{r,V_{1}}\big(\lVert(f_{[2]})_{z}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r,\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=C_{r,V_{1},1}\big(\lVert f_{[2]}\rVert\big)^{2}+C_{r,V_{1}}\big(\lVert f_{[2]}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{1})}{\leq}C_{r,V_{1},1}\lVert f\rVert^{2}+C_{r,V_{1}}\big(\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{r,V_{1},2}\big(\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r}\big)^{2}\,.

We explain steps.

•

(x)
: we recall that
E=V_{1}\oplus V
and then use Sobolev embedding theorem.

•

(y)
: from (
7.5
), we see that for any
v\in V
, any
\xi\in W^{r,\,\exp(E)}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))
and any
n\geq 0

\displaystyle v^{n}\xi=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\,(z\cdot v)n}\xi_{z}.

Thus, for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}
, if
(f_{E,\bot})_{z}\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\lVert(f_{[2]})_{z}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r,\mathcal{H}_{z}}\leq C_{r,V_{1}}\lVert(f_{E,\bot})_{z}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}.

•

(x_{1})
: we use (
7.11
).

Hence, we have

(7.12)

\displaystyle\lVert f_{[2]}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\leq C_{r,V_{1}}\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r}.

It is clear that

(7.13)

\displaystyle f_{E,\bot}=f_{[1]}+f_{[2]}

and

(7.14)

\displaystyle f_{[i]}\in L_{E,\bot},\qquad i=1,\,2.

Step 4: Construction of
f_{[1],i}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim V

We define a map
\varsigma:\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}\to\{1,2,3,\cdots,\dim V\}
by assigning to each
z
an index
\varsigma(z)
such that

(7.15)

\displaystyle|z\cdot v_{\varsigma(z)}|\geq\frac{1}{\dim V}.

Note
. Although this index
\varsigma(z)
may not be unique for every
z
, one may always choose one.

We define formal vectors

\displaystyle f_{[1],i}=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}(f_{[1],i})_{z},\qquad 1\leq i\leq\dim V,

by setting, for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}

(f_{[1],i})_{z}=\begin{cases}(f_{[1]})_{z},&\text{if }\varsigma(z)=i,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(
7.14
) shows that

(7.16)

\displaystyle(f_{[1],i})_{0}=0,\qquad 1\leq i\leq\dim V.

From the decomposition (
7.8
), for any
r\geq 0
we have

(7.17)

\displaystyle\lVert f_{[1],i}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\leq\lVert f_{[1]}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\overset{(x)}{\leq}\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(E),r}

for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
. Here in
(x)
we use (
7.11
).

It is clear that

(7.18)

\displaystyle f_{[1]}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V}f_{[1],i}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{[1],i}\in L_{E,\bot},\quad 1\leq i\leq\dim V.

Step 5: Solving for
\varphi_{[1],i,n}

Now, for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
we solve the equation:

(7.19)

\displaystyle v_{i}^{n}\varphi_{[1],i,n}=f_{[1],i}

which are equivalently to solve:

\displaystyle(2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot v_{i})^{n}(\varphi_{[1],i,n})_{z}=(f_{[1],i})_{z},\qquad\forall\,z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}.

Thus, we define

\displaystyle(\varphi_{[1],i,n})_{z}=\begin{cases}\frac{(f_{[1],i})_{z}}{(2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot v_{i})^{n}}\qquad&\text{if }0\neq z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E},\\
0\qquad&\text{if }z=0.\end{cases}

From (
7.16
) we see that

\displaystyle\varphi_{[1],i,n}=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}(\varphi_{[1],i,n})_{z}

is a solution of the equation (
7.19
).

To estimate
(\varphi_{[1],i,n})_{z}
, we note that if
(f_{[1],i})_{z}\neq 0
, then

(7.20)

\displaystyle(f_{[1],i})_{z}=(f_{[1]})_{z};\quad\text{and}\quad z\cdot v_{i}=z\cdot v_{\varsigma(z)}.

Thus, if
(f_{i})_{z}\neq 0
, then

(7.21)

\displaystyle|z\cdot v_{i}|=|z\cdot v_{\varsigma(z)}|\overset{(x)}{\geq}\frac{1}{\dim V}

Here in
(x)
we use (
7.15
).

It follows that

\displaystyle\|(\varphi_{[1],i,n})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|(f_{[1]})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}}{|2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot v_{i}|^{n}}\overset{(x)}{\leq}C_{n}\|(f_{[1]})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}.

Here in
(x)
we use (
7.21
).

Hence, for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
we have

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{[1],i,n}\rVert

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\big(\|(\varphi_{[1],i,n})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\big(C_{n}\|(f_{[1]})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

(7.22)

\displaystyle=C_{n}\|f_{[1]}\|\overset{(x)}{\leq}C_{n}\|f\|

and thus

(7.23)

\displaystyle\varphi_{[1],i,n}\in L_{E,\bot}.

Here in
(x)
we use (
7.11
).

Step 6: Construction of
f_{[2],i}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim V

Since
V\subset E
is a Diophantine subspace, for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}\backslash\{0\}
, we have

(7.24)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\dim V}|z\cdot v_{j}|\geq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}}\lVert z\rVert^{-\dim E},

where
\lVert\cdot\rVert
is induced by the Euclidean distance (see (
4.6
) of Section
4.3.2
).

We define a map
\varsigma:\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}\to\{1,2,3,\cdots,\dim V\}
by assigning to each
z
an index
\varsigma(z)
such that

(7.25)

\displaystyle|z\cdot v_{\varsigma(z)}|\geq\frac{1}{\dim V}\sum_{j=1}^{\dim V}|z\cdot v_{j}|.

Note
. Although this index
\varsigma(z)
may not be unique for every
z
, one may always choose one.

We define formal vectors

\displaystyle f_{[2],i}=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}(f_{[2],i})_{z},\qquad 1\leq i\leq\dim V,

by setting, for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}

(f_{[2],i})_{z}=\begin{cases}(f_{[2]})_{z},&\text{if }\varsigma(z)=i,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(
7.14
) shows that

(7.26)

\displaystyle(f_{[2],i})_{0}=0,\qquad 1\leq i\leq\dim V.

From the decomposition (
7.8
), for any
r\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\lVert f_{[2],i}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert f_{[2]}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}

(7.27)

\displaystyle\overset{(x)}{\leq}\min\{C_{r,V_{1}}\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{1}),r},\,\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\}

for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
. Here in
(x)
we use (
7.11
) and (
7.12
).

It is clear that

(7.28)

\displaystyle f_{[2]}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V}f_{[2],i}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{[2],i}\in L_{E,\bot},\quad 1\leq i\leq\dim V.

Step 7: Solving for
\varphi_{[2],i,n}

Now, for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
we solve the equation:

(7.29)

\displaystyle v_{i}^{n}\varphi_{[2],i,n}=f_{[2],i}

which are equivalently to solve:

\displaystyle(2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot v_{i})^{n}(\varphi_{[2],i,n})_{z}=(f_{[2],i})_{z},\qquad\forall\,z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}.

Thus, we define

\displaystyle(\varphi_{[2],i,n})_{z}=\begin{cases}\frac{(f_{[2],i})_{z}}{(2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot v_{i})^{n}}\qquad&\text{if }0\neq z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E},\\
0\qquad&\text{if }z=0.\end{cases}

From (
7.26
) we see that

\displaystyle\varphi_{[2],i,n}=\oplus_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}(\varphi_{[2],i,n})_{z}

is a solution of the equation (
7.29
).

To estimate
(\varphi_{[2],i,n})_{z}
, we note that if
(f_{[2],i})_{z}\neq 0
, then

(7.30)

\displaystyle(f_{[2],i})_{z}=(f_{[2]})_{z};\quad\text{and}\quad z\cdot v_{i}=z\cdot v_{\varsigma(z)}.

Thus, if
(f_{[2],i})_{z}\neq 0
, then for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}\backslash\{0\}
we have

(7.31)

\displaystyle|z\cdot v_{i}|=|z\cdot v_{\varsigma(z)}|\overset{(x)}{\geq}\frac{1}{\dim V}\sum_{j=1}^{\dim V}|z\cdot v_{j}|\overset{(y)}{\geq}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}}\lVert z\rVert^{-\dim E}.

Here in
(x)
we use (
7.25
); in
(y)
we use (
7.24
).

It follows that

\displaystyle\|(\varphi_{[2],i,n})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|(f_{[2],i})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}}{|2\pi\mathbf{i}\,z\cdot v_{i}|^{n}}\overset{(x)}{\leq}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n}\|(f_{[2],i})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\cdot\lVert z\rVert^{n\dim E}

\displaystyle\overset{(y)}{=}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n}\|(f_{[2]})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\cdot\lVert z\rVert^{n\dim E}

for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}\backslash\{0\}
.

Here in
(x)
we use (
7.31
); in
(y)
we use (
7.30
).

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{[2],i,n}\rVert

\displaystyle=\Big(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\big(\|(\varphi_{i,n})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n}\Big(\sum_{z\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}}\big(\|(f_{[2]})_{z}\|_{\mathcal{H}_{z}}\big)^{2}\cdot\lVert z\rVert^{2n\dim E}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\overset{(x)}{\leq}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n,1}\big\|(\Delta_{E})^{\frac{n\dim E}{2}}f_{[2]}\big\|

\displaystyle\overset{(y)}{\leq}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n,2}\big\|f_{[2]}\big\|_{\exp(E),n\dim E}

(7.32)

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{1})}{\leq}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n,2,V_{1}}\big\|f\big\|_{\exp(V_{1}),n\dim E}

and thus

(7.33)

\displaystyle\varphi_{[2],i,n}\in L_{E,\bot}.

Here in
(x)
we recall (
7.6
); in
(y)
we use (
7.7
); in
(x_{1})
we use (
7.12
).

Step 8: Construction of
f_{i}
and
\varphi_{i,n}

For any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, let

\displaystyle f_{i}=f_{[1],i}+f_{[2],i}\quad\text{and}\quad\varphi_{i,n}=\varphi_{[1],i,n}+\varphi_{[2],i,n}.

It follows from (
7.13
), (
7.18
) and (
7.28
) that

\displaystyle f_{E,\bot}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V}f_{i}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{i}\in L_{E,\bot},\quad 1\leq i\leq\dim V.

Moreover, from (
7.17
) and (
8.15
), we have

\displaystyle\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{\exp(E),r}\leq C\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(E),r},\qquad\forall\,r\geq 0;

Thus, we get (
1
).

From (
7.19
) and (
7.29
) we see that
for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
, the equations

\displaystyle v_{i}^{n}\varphi_{i,n}=f_{i}.

Moreover, from (
7.2
) and (
7.2
) that

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{i,n}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\varphi_{[1],i,n}\rVert+\lVert\varphi_{[2],i,n}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{n}\|f\|+C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n,V_{1}}\big\|f\big\|_{\exp(V_{1}),n\dim E}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},n,V_{1},1}\big\|f\big\|_{\exp(V_{1}),n\dim E}\,.

Finally, from (
7.23
) and (
7.33
) we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{i,n}\in L_{E,\bot},\qquad\forall\,n\in\mathbb{N},\text{ and }1\leq i\leq\dim V.

Then we get (
2
). Then we complete the proof.

∎

At the end of this section, we list some results that will be used in the subsequent part. We recall notations in Sections
1
and
2
. The proof is left for Appendix
E
.

Lemma 7.2
.

For any
f,\,g\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
and an automorphism
\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{X}
, we have:

(1)

\int_{\mathcal{X}}g\cdot f_{E,o}d\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}}g_{E,o}\cdot f_{E,o}d\varrho
.

(2)

\int_{\mathcal{X}}g_{E,\bot}\cdot f_{E,o}d\varrho=0
.

(3)

f_{E,o}\cdot g_{E,\bot}=(f_{E,o}\cdot g)_{E,\bot}
.

(4)

For any subalgebra
V\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
and
s\geq 0
, if
f\in W^{\exp(V),s}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))
, then:

(a)

f_{E,o}\in W^{\exp(V),s}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))
with the estimate

\displaystyle\big\|f_{E,o}\big\|_{\exp(V),s}\leq\|f\|_{\exp(V),s}.

(b)

We use
\mathfrak{i}
to denote the projection from
\mathfrak{n}
to
\mathfrak{n}/E
. Then

f_{E,o}\in W^{\exp(\mathfrak{i}(V)),s}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}_{E}))
with the estimate

\displaystyle\big\|f_{E,o}\big\|_{\exp(\mathfrak{i}(V)),s}\leq\|f\|_{\exp(V),s}.

(5)

Suppose
E
is
d\mathcal{A}
-invariant.
Define

\displaystyle f\circ\mathcal{A}(x)=f(\mathcal{A}x),\qquad\forall\,x\in\mathcal{X}.

Then

(f\circ\mathcal{A})_{E,o}\in L_{E,o}
, and moreover

\displaystyle(f\circ\mathcal{A})_{E,o}=f_{E,o}\circ\mathcal{A}.

(6)

We assume notations in (
5
). Then

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}g\circ\mathcal{A}\,\cdot f_{E,o}\,d\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{E}}g_{E,o}\circ\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{X}_{E}}\,\cdot f_{E,o}\,d\varrho_{1,E}.

(7)

\int_{\mathcal{X}}gd\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{E}}g_{E,o}d\varrho_{1,E}
.

(8)

if
f\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{N})
, for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
, any
n\in\mathbb{N}
and any
v\in E_{\chi}
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert v^{n}(f(\alpha(z)))\rVert_{C^{0}}\leq C_{n}e^{n\chi(z)}(1+\lVert z\rVert)^{nl\dim E}\lVert v\rVert\lVert f\rVert_{C^{n}}

Remark 7.3
.

(
5
) shows that
f_{E,o}(\mathcal{A}x)
is also constant along the torus bundle
\mathbb{T}_{E}
.

8.
Study of codicological equations, nilpotent case II

8.1.
Notations

We list frequently used notations in this section.

1
.

Let
V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
denote the linear space spanned by
\mathcal{E}^{1}=\{E_{1}^{1},E_{2}^{1},\cdots,E_{n_{1}}^{1}\}
(see Section
4.3.1
), where

n_{1}=\dim\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
.
\mathcal{E}^{1}
determines a
\mathbb{Z}^{n_{1}}
lattice in
V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
. We use
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}
to denote natural projection from
\mathfrak{n}
to
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
. Suppose
E\subset V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace (see Section
4.3.2
). We say that
E

is
effective
if

\displaystyle[E,\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}]\neq\{0\}.

In that case, we denote

\displaystyle E^{[1]}=[E,\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}].

2
.

We say that a subspace
V\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
is a
Diophantine subspace for
E\subset V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
of type
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
if there is a Diophantine subspace
F
of
E
(see Section
4.3.2
) such that
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(V)=\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(F)
.

3
.

For a subspace
V\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
, we say that a subspace
V_{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}

is dual to
V
if the following conditions are satisfied:

(1)

there is a subspace
W\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
such that
\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}=V_{1}\oplus W
;

(2)

for any
u\in\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
, if there exists
v\in V
such that
[u,v]\neq 0
, then the
V_{1}
-component
u_{V_{1}}
in the unique decomposition

u=u_{V_{1}}+u_{W},\quad\text{with }u_{V_{1}}\in V_{1}\text{ and }u_{W}\in W,

is nonzero.

Note
. It is clear from the definition that
[V,\,W]=0
.

We say that a subspace
V_{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}

is minimally dual to
V
if for any subspace
P\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
which is dual to
V
, we have
V_{1}\subseteq P
.

Lemma 8.1
.

For any subspace
V\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
