, we have:

(1)

a dual subspace
V_{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
always exists;

(2)

if
W
satisfies (
1
), then it is unique;

(3)

the minimal dual space of
V
always exists and is unique.

Since the proof is straightforward, we omit it.

4
.

(i) Weakly integral (with respect to
\Gamma
): see Definition
8.4
.

(ii)
\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)
,
\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X)
and
B_{\lambda}(X,Y)
: see Section
8.3.2
.

(iii) Maximal rank: see Section
8.3.2
.

(iv) Coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}_{\ell}
respects
E^{[1]}
: see Section
8.3.3
.

(V)
\mathcal{O}_{\ell}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\ell}
,
\ell\in\Sigma
: see Section
8.3.3
.

8.2.
Main result

Theorem 8.2
.

Suppose

(1)

E\subset V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace;

(2)

V\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
is a Diophantine subspace for
E
of type
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
. We fix a basis
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}\}
of
V
;

(3)

V_{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
is minimally dual to
V
;

(4)

V_{2}
denotes algebra generated by
V_{1}
and
E^{[1]}=[E,\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}]
and
\exp(V_{2})
denotes the subgroup of
N
with its Lie algebra being
V_{2}
.

Then, for any
f\in W^{\dim E+1,\,\exp(V_{2})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))
we can write

\displaystyle f_{E^{[1]},\bot}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V}f_{i}

where
f_{i}\in L_{E^{[1]},\bot}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim V
(see (
1
) of Section
7.1
) such that the following are satisfied:

(1)

for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
we have

\displaystyle\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1}\leq C\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1};

(2)

for any
0<a<\frac{1}{2}
and any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
, the equation

\displaystyle|v_{i}|^{a}(\varphi_{i})=f_{i}

has a solution
\varphi_{i}\in L_{E^{[1]},\bot}
(see (
1
) of Section
7.1
) with the estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{i}\rVert\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},a}\,\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1}.

Remark 8.3
.

If
E
is not effective, then
E^{[1]}=\{0\}
, so
f_{E^{[1]},\bot}=0
. In that case, the result holds trivially.

8.3.
Preliminaries on nilmanifolds

8.3.1.
Representation of nilpotent groups

Let
N
be a simply connected nilpotent group with Lie algebra
\mathfrak{n}
. By Kirillov theory, all the irreducible
unitary representation of
N
are parametrized by the
coadjoint orbits

\mathcal{O}\subset\mathfrak{n}^{*}
, i.e. by the orbits of the
coadjoint action
of
N
on

\mathfrak{n}^{*}
defined by

\displaystyle\text{Ad}^{*}(g)\lambda=\lambda\circ\text{Ad}(g^{-1}),\qquad g\in N,\,\lambda\in\mathfrak{n}^{*}.

For
\lambda\in\mathfrak{n}^{*}
, the skew-symmetric bilinear form

(8.1)

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}(X,Y)=\lambda([X,Y])

has a
radical

\tau_{\lambda}
which coincides with the Lie subalgebra of the subgroup
of
N
stabilizing
\lambda
; thus the form (
8.1
) is non-degenerate on the tangent space
to the orbit
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathfrak{n}^{*}
and defines a symplectic form on
\mathcal{O}
.
A polarizing (or maximal subordinate) subalgebra for
\lambda
is a maximal
isotropic subspace
\mathfrak{m}\subset\mathfrak{n}
for the form
B_{\lambda}
which is also a subalgebra of
\mathfrak{n}
.
In particular any polarizing subalgebra for the linear form
\lambda
contains the
radical
\tau_{\lambda}
. If
\mathfrak{m}
is a polarizing subalgebra for the linear form
\lambda
, the map

\displaystyle\exp t\to e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\lambda(t)},\qquad t\in\mathfrak{m},

yields a one-dimensional representation, which we denote by
e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\lambda(t)}
, of
the subgroup
M=\exp(\mathfrak{m})\subset N
.

To a pair
\Lambda:=(\lambda,\mathfrak{m})
formed by a linear form
\lambda\in\mathfrak{n}^{*}
and a polarizing
subalgebra
\mathfrak{m}
for
\lambda
, we associate the unitary representation

\displaystyle\pi_{\Lambda}=\text{Ind}_{\exp(\mathfrak{m})}^{N}e^{2\pi\mathbf{i}\lambda(t)}

These unitary representations are irreducible; up to unitary equivalence, all
unitary irreducible representations of
N
are obtained in this way. Furthermore,
two pairs
\Lambda:=(\lambda,\mathfrak{m})
and
\Lambda^{\prime}:=(\lambda^{\prime},\mathfrak{m}^{\prime})
yield unitarily equivalent
representations
\pi_{\Lambda}
and
\pi_{\Lambda^{\prime}}
if and only if
\Lambda
and
\Lambda^{\prime}
belong to the same coadjoint
orbit
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathfrak{n}^{*}
.

The unitary equivalence class of the representations of the group
N
determined
by the coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}
will be denoted by
\Pi_{\mathcal{O}}
, while we set

\displaystyle\Pi_{\lambda}=\{\pi_{\lambda}|\,\Lambda=(\lambda,\mathfrak{m}),\,\text{with $\mathfrak{m}$ polarizing subalgebra for }\lambda\}.

Definition 8.4
.

A linear form
\lambda\in\mathfrak{n}^{*}
is called
weakly integral (with respect to
\Gamma
) if
\lambda(Y)\in\mathbb{Z}
for all
Y\in\log Z(\Gamma)
. A
coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}\subset\mathfrak{n}^{*}
will be called weakly integral (with respect to
\Gamma
) if
some
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
is weakly integral (hence all
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
are).

Let
\Gamma
be a discrete cocompact
subgroup of
N
and
\mathcal{X}=N/\Gamma
. Denote by
\varrho
the Haar measure of
\mathcal{X}
. The Hilbert space
L^{2}(\mathcal{X},d\varrho)
decomposes under the left action of
N
into
a countable Hilbert sum
\bigoplus_{i}\mathcal{H}_{i}
of irreducible closed subspaces
\mathcal{H}_{i}
. The Howe-Richardson multiplicity formula tells us that

Lemma 8.5
.

All irreducible unitary subrepresentations
occurring in
L^{2}(\mathcal{X},d\varrho)
correspond to those induced by Kirillov’s
by weakly integral coadjoint orbits.

8.3.2.
Maximal rank

Let
\lambda\in\mathfrak{n}^{*}
and let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\{Y\in\mathfrak{n}|\,B_{\lambda}(Y,\mathfrak{n}_{k-1})=0\},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\mathcal{O})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\lambda),\qquad\text{for any }\lambda\in\mathcal{O}.

Denote by
Z(\mathfrak{n})
is the centre of
\mathfrak{n}
. Since
\lambda|_{Z(\mathfrak{n})}
does not depend on
the choice of the linear form
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
and since
[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}]=\mathfrak{n}_{k}\subset Z(\mathfrak{n})
,
the restriction
B_{\lambda}|_{\mathfrak{n}\times\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}}
and the subspace
\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\lambda)
depend only on the
coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}
. Consequently,
\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\mathcal{O})
is well-posed.

Lemma 8.6
.

(Lemma 2.3. of
[
7
]
) Let
\mathcal{O}
be a coadjoint orbit and
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
. The following properties
are equivalent:

(1)

the restriction
\lambda|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
is identically zero;

(2)

\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\mathcal{O})=\mathfrak{n}
.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a coadjoint orbit and
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
. If the restriction
\lambda|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
is not identically zero, we will say that the coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}
, any linear form
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
and any
irreducible representation
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{O}}
have
maximal rank
(equal to
k
).

For all
(X,Y)\in\mathfrak{n}\times\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
, let

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)

\displaystyle:=|B_{\lambda}(X,Y)|,\qquad\text{for any }\lambda\in\mathcal{O},\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X)

\displaystyle:=\max\{\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)|\,Y\in\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}\text{ and }\lVert Y\rVert=1\}.

Note
: Since
[X,Y]\in Z(\mathfrak{n})
, the value
\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)
does not depend on
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
.

By definition we have
\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X)>0
if and only if
X\notin\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\mathcal{O})
. The latter
condition is non-empty if and only if
\mathcal{O}
has maximal rank, in which case it
holds except for a subspace of positive codimension.

Let
\mathcal{O}
be a coadjoint orbit and
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
. If the restriction
\lambda|_{E^{[1]}}
(recall (
1
) of Section
8.1
) is not identically zero, we will say that the coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}
, any linear form
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
and any
irreducible representation
\pi\in\Pi_{\mathcal{O}}

respect
E^{[1]}
.

Remark 8.7
.

Since
E^{[1]}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k}
, if the coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}_{\ell}
respects
E^{[1]}
, then
\mathcal{O}
has maximal rank.

8.3.3.
Decomposition of
\pi

\pi
is the left action of
N
on
L^{2}(\mathcal{X},d\varrho)
. Let

(8.2)

\displaystyle L^{2}(\mathcal{X},d\varrho)=\bigoplus_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}\mathcal{H}_{\ell}

be the decomposition
into irreducible representations of
N
. Let
\pi_{\ell}\in\prod_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}
be an irreducible representation
of
N
on
\mathcal{H}_{\ell}
.

We consider the restricted representation
\pi|_{L_{E^{[1]},\bot}}
(see (
1
) of Section
7.1
), i.e., the left action of
N
on

L_{E^{[1]},\bot}
. Then we have a decomposition

(8.3)

\displaystyle\pi|_{L_{E^{[1]},\bot}}=\bigoplus_{\ell\in\Sigma}\mathcal{H}_{\ell}

where
\Sigma
is a subset of
\mathbb{N}
. We note that

(1)

each
\mathcal{O}_{\ell}
respects
E^{[1]}
(see the end of Section
8.3.2
);

(2)

each
\mathcal{O}_{\ell}
is a non-trivial weakly integral coadjoint orbit (with respect to
\Gamma
) (see Definition
8.4
).

In fact, (
2
) of Lemma
7.2
shows that the subspaces
L_{E^{[1]},\bot}
and
L_{E^{[1]},o}
are orthogonal to each other. We note that
L_{E^{[1]},o}
contains all
E^{[1]}
-invariant vectors. Then there are no nontrivial
E^{[1]}
-invariant vectors in
L_{E^{[1]},\bot}
. This implies (
1
). Lemma
8.5
implies (
2
).

The decomposition (
8.2
) induces a decomposition of the subspace of the Sobolev spaces (see Section
4.1
): for each
r\in\mathbb{R}
, there is an orthogonal splitting

(8.4)

\displaystyle W^{r,\,\exp(V_{2})}(L_{E^{[1]},\bot})=\bigoplus_{\ell\in\Sigma}W^{r,\,\exp(V_{2})}(\mathcal{H}_{\ell}).

8.3.4.
Fractional cohomological equation

The following lemma (see Lemma
2.5
of
[
7
]
) of L. Flaminio and G. Forni provides, for any coadjoint orbit
\mathcal{O}
of maximal
rank and any
X\in\mathfrak{n}\backslash\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\mathcal{O})
, a special irreducible unitary representation
adapted to the analysis of the fractional cohomological equation.

Lemma 8.8
.

Let
X\in\mathfrak{n}\backslash\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\mathcal{O})
and let
Y\in\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
be any element such
that
B_{\lambda}(X,Y)\neq 0
for all
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
. There exists a unitary (irreducible) representation
\pi\in\prod_{\mathcal{O}}
with
the following properties:

(1)

the associated Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
with
\pi
is
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}=L^{2}(\mathbb{R},H^{\prime},dt)
where
H^{\prime}
is a Hilbert space;

(2)

the derived the representation
\pi^{*}
of the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle X\xi=\text{\tiny$\frac{\partial}{\partial t}$}\xi\quad\text{and}\quad Y\xi=2\pi\mathbf{i}B_{\lambda}(X,Y)t\cdot\xi

for any
\xi\in W^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\pi})
.

Lemma 8.9
.

We assume notations as in Lemma
8.8
. Then for any
\xi\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
with
Y\xi\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
and any
0<a<\frac{1}{2}
, the fractional cohomological equation

\displaystyle|X|^{a}u=\xi;

has a solution
u\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
with the estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\omega\rVert\leq C_{a}\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)^{-1}\lVert Y\psi\rVert+C\lVert\psi\rVert.

Note
.
\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)=|B_{\lambda}(X,Y)|
(see Section
8.3.2
).

Proof.

Set
W=[X,Y]
. A straightforward computation shows that

\displaystyle W\xi=(XY-YX)\xi=2\pi\mathbf{i}B_{\lambda}(X,Y)\cdot\xi.

We note that the elements
X
,
Y
, and
W
generate a Heisenberg algebra, and hence a Heisenberg group
\mathbb{H}
. We have a direct integral decomposition of
\pi
:

\displaystyle\pi=\int_{Z}\rho_{z}dz,

where
\{\rho_{z}\}_{z\in Z}
is a family of irreducible unitary representations of

\mathbb{H}
over a measure space
(Z,\mu)
(see Section
5.1
). In each
\rho_{z}
,
W
acts by multiplication by the non-zero constant
2\pi\mathbf{i}B_{\lambda}(X,Y)
in each
\rho_{z}
.

By Mackey theory, such irreducible unitary representations are all equivalent to the unitary representation
(\tau,\mathcal{H}_{\tau})
, where
\mathcal{H}_{\tau}=L^{2}(\mathbb{R},dx)
and the derived the representation
\tau^{*}
satisfies:

(8.5)

\displaystyle X\psi=\mathbf{i}x\psi,\quad Y\psi=-2\pi B_{\lambda}(X,Y)\text{\tiny$\frac{\partial}{\partial x}$}\psi\,\text{ and }\,W\xi=2\pi\mathbf{i}B_{\lambda}(X,Y)\cdot\psi

for any
\psi\in W^{\infty}(\mathcal{H}_{\tau})
.

Thus, to prove the result, it suffices to prove the following: for any
\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
with
Y\psi\in\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
, the equation

\displaystyle|x|^{a}\omega=\psi

has a solution
\omega\in\mathcal{H}_{\tau}
with the estimate:

\displaystyle\lVert\omega\rVert\leq C_{a}\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)^{-1}\lVert Y\psi\rVert+C\lVert\psi\rVert.

We set

\displaystyle\omega=\frac{\psi}{|x|^{a}}.

By Sobolev embedding theorem, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\psi\rVert_{C^{0}(\mathbb{R})}\leq C(\|\text{\tiny$\frac{\partial}{\partial x}$}\psi\|+\lVert\psi\rVert)=C\big(\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)^{-1}\lVert Y\psi\rVert+\lVert\psi\rVert\big).

It follows that

\displaystyle\lVert\omega\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\psi(x)|^{2}|x|^{-2a}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{|x|\leq 1}|\psi(x)|^{2}|x|^{-2a}dx+\int_{|x|>1}|\psi(x)|^{2}|x|^{-2a}dx

\displaystyle\leq(\lVert\psi\rVert_{C^{0}(\mathbb{R})})^{2}\int_{|x|\leq 1}|x|^{-2a}dx+\int_{|x|>1}|\psi(x)|^{2}dx

\displaystyle\leq C_{a}(\lVert\psi\rVert_{C^{0}(\mathbb{R})})^{2}+\lVert\psi\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{a}\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(X,Y)^{-2}\lVert Y\psi\rVert^{2}+C_{1}\lVert\psi\rVert^{2},

where the condition
0<a<\frac{1}{2}
ensures that
\int_{|x|\leq 1}|x|^{-2a}dx
converges.

Then we complete the proof.

∎

8.4.
Diophantine property

Suppose
V\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
is a Diophantine subspace for
E
of type
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
and
V_{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
the minimal dual space of
V
(see (
3
) of Lemma
8.1
).
We fix a basis

\displaystyle\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}\}\quad\text{of }V.

For any
\ell\in\Sigma
, any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
, define
\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})
as follows:

(8.6)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i}):=\max\{\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i},Y)|\,Y\in V_{1}\text{ and }\lVert Y\rVert=1\}

(recall that
\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i},Y)
is defined in Section
8.3.2
).

Proposition 8.10
.

For any
\ell\in\Sigma
, there exists
1\leq j\leq\dim V
such that
\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{j})>0
and

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{j})^{-1}\lVert\xi\rVert\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}}\big\|\xi\big\|_{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E}

for any
\xi\in W^{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E}(\mathcal{H}_{\ell})
.

Lemma 8.11
.

For any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
and any
\ell\in\Sigma
, we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})\leq\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})\leq C_{V}\,\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i}).

Proof.

By definition, we have

(8.7)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})\leq\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i}).

If
\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})=0
, it is clear that

(8.8)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})\leq\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i}).

Otherwise, choose
Y_{\ell,i}\in\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
with
\lVert Y_{\ell,i}\rVert=1
such that

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})=\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i},Y_{\ell,i})>0.

By definition,
[v_{i},Y_{\ell,i}]\neq 0
. The duality of
V_{1}
implies that

\displaystyle[v_{i},(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}]\neq 0\quad\text{and }\quad[v_{i},(Y_{\ell,i})_{W}]=0,

where
Y_{\ell,i}=(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}+(Y_{\ell,i})_{W}
is the unique decomposition corresponding to the direct sum

\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}=V_{1}\oplus W.

(see (
3
) of Section
8.1
). Thus, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})

\displaystyle=\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}\big(v_{i},(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}\big)=\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}\big(v_{i},\frac{(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}}{\lVert(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}\rVert}\big)\cdot\lVert(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}\rVert

\displaystyle\overset{(x)}{\leq}\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})\cdot\lVert(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}\rVert\overset{(y)}{\leq}C_{V}\,\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i}).

Here in
(x)
we note that the unit vector
\frac{(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}}{\lVert(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}\rVert}
lies in
V_{1}
, by the definition of
\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i})
we have

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}\Bigl(v_{i},\frac{(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}}{\|(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}\|}\Bigr)\leq\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i}).

In
(y)
we the use fact that

\displaystyle\lVert u_{V_{1}}\rVert+\lVert u_{W}\rVert\leq C_{V}\lVert u\rVert,\qquad\forall\,u\in\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}

and note that
\|Y_{\ell,i}\|=1
. Thus, it follows that

\|(Y_{\ell,i})_{V_{1}}\|\leq C_{V}\lVert Y_{\ell,i}\rVert=C_{V}.

Then we see that (
8.8
) still holds. (
8.7
) and (
8.8
) implies the result.

∎

Lemma 8.12
.

Suppose

(1)

E\subset\mathcal{E}^{1}
is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace and
F
is a Diophantine subspace of
E
(see (
2
)). Fix a basis
\{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}\}
of
F
.

(2)

\mathcal{O}
is a coadjoint orbit which respects
E^{[1]}
(see Section
8.3.3
) and
weakly integral (with respect to
\Gamma
) (see Definition
8.4
).

(3)

\pi\in\prod_{\mathcal{O}}
.

Then, there exist
Y_{0}\in\mathcal{E}^{k-1}
(see Section
4.3.1
) and an index
1\leq j_{0}\leq\dim F
such that:

(1)

\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(u_{j_{0}})>0
;

(2)

for any
\xi\in W^{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E}(\mathcal{H}_{\pi})
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(u_{j_{0}})^{-1}\lVert\xi\rVert\leq C_{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}}\big\|\xi\big\|_{\exp(E^{[1]}),\dim E}.

Proof.

Let
\lambda\in\mathcal{O}
and let
\{W_{1},\cdots,W_{\dim E}\}
be an integer basis of
E
. Since
F
is a subspace of
E
,
for the basis
\{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}\}
of
F
, we can write

\displaystyle u_{j}=x_{j,1}W_{1}+x_{i,2}W_{2}\cdots+x_{j,\dim E}W_{\dim E},\qquad 1\leq j\leq\dim F.

Since
F
is also Diophantine, for any
z=(z_{1},\cdots,z_{n_{1}})\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}
, we have

(8.9)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\dim F}|z\cdot u_{j}|:=\sum_{j=1}^{\dim F}\big|\sum_{i=1}^{\dim E}z_{i}x_{j,i}\big|\geq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim F}}\lVert z\rVert^{-\dim E}.

Step 1: We show that:
for any
Y\in\mathcal{E}^{k-1}

\displaystyle z_{Y}:=\big(B_{\lambda}(W_{1},Y),\cdots,B_{\lambda}(W_{\dim E},Y)\big)

lies inside
\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}
.

We recall that
\mathcal{E}^{k-1}\subset\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
. Then for any
Y\in\mathcal{E}^{k-1}
and any
1\leq i\leq\dim E
, we have

\displaystyle[W_{i},\,Y]\in\mathfrak{n}_{k}.

It follows from the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, we have

\displaystyle\exp[W_{i},\,Y]=[\exp W_{j},\exp Y]\in Z(\Gamma).

This shows that
[W_{i},\,Y]\in\log Z(\Gamma)
. Since
\lambda
is weakly integral, we have

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}(W_{i},\,Y)=\lambda[W_{i},\,Y]\in\mathbb{Z}

for any
Y\in\mathcal{E}^{k-1}
and any
1\leq i\leq\dim E
.

Then we get the result.

Step 2: We show that:
there exists
Y_{0}\in\mathcal{E}^{k-1}
such that

\displaystyle z_{Y_{0}}\in\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}\backslash 0.

We recall that
\mathcal{E}^{k-1}\cup\mathcal{E}^{k}
form a basis of
\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
and
\mathcal{E}^{k}
form a basis of
\mathfrak{n}_{k}
(see Section
4.3.1
). It is clear that

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}(W_{i},\,v)=0,\qquad\forall\,v\in\mathfrak{n}_{k}.

If for all
Y\in\mathcal{E}^{k-1}
and and all
1\leq i\leq\dim E
, we have

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}(W_{i},\,Y)=0,

then the above discussion implies that

\displaystyle B_{\lambda}(W_{i},\,v)=0,\qquad\forall\,v\in\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}.

Thus,
\lambda|_{E^{[1]}}
is identically zero (recall that
E^{[1]}=[E,\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}]
),
contradicting the assumption that
\lambda
respects
E^{[1]}
. Then the claim is proved.

Step 3: We show that:
there exists
1\leq j_{0}\leq\dim F
such that

(8.10)

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(u_{j_{0}})\geq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim F}}\lVert z_{Y_{0}}\rVert^{-\dim E}.

In fact, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\dim F}\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(u_{j})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=1}^{\dim F}\delta_{\mathcal{O}}\big(u_{j},\frac{Y_{0}}{\lVert Y_{0}\rVert}\big)=\lVert Y_{0}\rVert^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\dim F}|B_{\lambda}(u_{j},Y_{0})|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(a)}}{=}\lVert Y_{0}\rVert^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{\dim F}|z_{Y_{0}}\cdot u_{j}|

\displaystyle\overset{(a)}{\geq}C_{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}}\lVert z_{Y_{0}}\rVert^{-\dim E}.

Here in
(a)
we use (
8.9
). The above inequality implies (
8.10
).

Step 4
Finally, set

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{O}}=-(2\pi)^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{\dim E}[W_{i},Y_{0}]^{2}\in\mathcal{U}(E^{[1]}),

where
\mathcal{U}(E^{[1]})
denotes the universal enveloping algebra of
E^{[1]}
.

Since

[W_{i},Y_{0}]\in Z(\mathfrak{n})
,
1\leq j\leq\dim E
, we have

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{O}}(\xi)=\lVert z_{Y_{0}}\rVert^{2}\xi,\qquad\forall\,\xi\in\mathcal{H}_{\pi}.

It follows from (
8.10
) that

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}}(u_{j_{0}})^{-1}\lVert\xi\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}}\lVert z_{Y_{0}}\rVert^{\dim E}\lVert\xi\rVert

\displaystyle=C_{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}}\big\|(P_{\mathcal{O}})^{\frac{\dim E}{2}}\xi\big\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}}\lVert\xi\rVert_{\exp(E^{[1]}),\dim E}.

for any
\xi\in W^{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E}(\mathcal{H}_{\pi})
.

Then we complete the proof.
∎

8.4.1.
Proof of Proposition
8.10

Since
V
is a Diophantine subspace for
E
of type
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
, there is a Diophantine subspace
F
of
E
such that
\mathfrak{p}(V)=\mathfrak{p}(F)
. We note that
\mathfrak{p}:V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}\to\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
(see (
1
) of Section
8.1
) is an isomorphism. Then for any
1\leq j\leq\dim V
, there is a unique
u_{j}\in F
such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}(v_{j})=\mathfrak{p}(u_{j}).

Then there exist
q_{j}\in[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
,
1\leq j\leq\dim V
such that

(8.11)

\displaystyle v_{j}=u_{j}+q_{j}.

Since
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}\}
is a basis of
V
,
\{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim V}\}
spans
F
. Hence, we may assume without loss of generality that
\{u_{1},\cdots,u_{\dim F}\}
is a basis of
F
.

From (
1
) and (
2
) of Section
8.3.3
, it follows from Lemma
8.12
that for any
\ell\in\Sigma
, there exists
an index
1\leq j\leq\dim F
such that

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(u_{j})^{-1}\lVert\xi\rVert\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}}\big\|\xi\big\|_{\exp(E^{[1]}),\dim E},

for any
\xi\in W^{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E}(\mathcal{H}_{\ell})
.

(
8.11
) shows that

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(u_{j})=\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{j}).

This together with Lemma
8.11
gives

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{j})^{-1}\lVert\xi\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{V}\,\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{j})^{-1}\lVert\xi\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}}\big\|\xi\big\|_{\exp(E^{[1]}),\dim E}.

for any
\xi\in W^{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E}(\mathcal{H}_{\ell})
.

Then we complete the proof.

8.5.
Proof of Theorem
8.2

We define a map

\displaystyle\varsigma:\Sigma\to\{1,2,3,\cdots,\dim V\}

by assigning to each
\ell\in\Sigma
an index
\varsigma(\ell)
such that
v_{\varsigma(\ell)}
is vector such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{\varsigma(\ell)})=\max_{1\leq i\dim V}\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i}),\qquad\forall\,\ell\in\Sigma.

Note
. Although this index
\varsigma(\ell)
may not be unique for every
\ell
, one may always choose one.

It follows from Proposition
8.10
that for any
\ell\in\Sigma

(8.12)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{\varsigma(\ell)})

\displaystyle>0

and

(8.13)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{\varsigma(\ell)})^{-1}\lVert\xi\rVert\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}}\big\|\xi\big\|_{\exp(E^{[1]}),\dim E}

for any
\xi\in W^{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E}(\mathcal{H}_{\ell})
.

For any
\ell\in\Sigma
, choose
Y_{\ell}\in V_{1}
with
\lVert Y_{l}\rVert=1
such that

(8.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{\delta}_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{\varsigma(\ell)})=\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{\varsigma(\ell)},Y_{\ell})

(recall (
8.6
)).

(
1
): Recall the decomposition (
8.3
) of Section
8.3.3
. We define formal vectors

\displaystyle f_{i}=\sum_{\ell\in\Sigma}(f_{i})_{\ell},\qquad 1\leq i\leq\dim V,

by setting, for any
\ell\in\Sigma

(f_{i})_{\ell}=\begin{cases}(f_{E^{[1]},\bot})_{\ell},&\text{if }\varsigma(\ell)=i,\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

From the decomposition (
8.4
) of Section
8.3.3
, for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
we have

(8.15)

\displaystyle\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert f_{E^{[1]},\bot}\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1}\leq\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1}.

It is clear that

\displaystyle f_{E^{[1]},\bot}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V}f_{i}

and
f_{i}\in L_{E^{[1]},\bot}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim V
. Then we get (
1
).

(
2
): Now, for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
we solve the equation:

(8.16)

\displaystyle|v_{i}|^{a}(\varphi_{i})=f_{i}

which are equivalently to solve:

\displaystyle|v_{i}|^{a}(\varphi_{i})_{\ell}=(f_{i})_{\ell},\qquad\ell\in\Sigma.

If
(f_{i})_{\ell}=0
, let

(8.17)

\displaystyle(\varphi_{i})_{\ell}=0.

Then it is clear that

(8.18)

\displaystyle|v_{i}|^{a}(\varphi_{i})_{\ell}=(f_{i})_{\ell}

If
(f_{i})_{\ell}\neq 0
, by definition,
\varsigma(\ell)=i
. Then:

(1)

Remark
8.7
and (
1
) of Section
8.3.3
show that
\mathcal{O}_{\ell}
has maximal rank;

(2)

(
8.12
) and (
8.14
) show that
v_{i}\in\mathfrak{n}\backslash\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}^{\bot}(\mathcal{O}_{\ell})
(see Section
8.3.2
) and

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i},Y_{\ell})=|B_{\lambda}(v_{i},\,Y_{\ell})|>0,\qquad\text{for any }\lambda\in\mathcal{O}_{\ell}.

Then it follows from Lemma
8.9
that the equation (
8.18
) has a solution
(\varphi_{i})_{\ell}\in\mathcal{H}_{\ell}
such that:

\displaystyle\lVert(\varphi_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}\leq C_{a}\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{\ell}}(v_{i},Y_{\ell})^{-1}\lVert Y_{\ell}(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}+C\lVert(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}.

Further, from (
8.13
) and (
8.14
) we have

\displaystyle\lVert(\varphi_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},a}\,\lVert Y_{\ell}(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\exp(E^{[1]}),\,\dim E,\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}+C\lVert(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},a}\,\lVert(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1,\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}+C\lVert(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}

(8.19)

\displaystyle\leq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},a,1}\,\lVert(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1,\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}.

Here in
(1)
we note that
Y_{\ell}\in V_{1}
.

Set

\displaystyle\varphi_{i}=\sum_{\ell\in\Sigma}(\varphi_{i})_{\ell},\qquad 1\leq i\leq\dim V.

Then it follows from (
8.17
) and (
8.5
) that for any
1\leq i\leq\dim V
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{i}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in\Sigma}(\lVert(\varphi_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\mathcal{H}_{\ell}})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell\in\Sigma}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},a}\big(\lVert(f_{i})_{\ell}\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1,\mathcal{H}_{\ell}}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},a}\big(\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V},a}\big(\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E+1}\big)^{2}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
8.15
).

It is clear that
\varphi_{i}\in L_{E^{[1]},\bot}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim V
. Hence we complete the proof.

9.
The proof of nilmanifolds case

9.1.
Notations and basic facts

We recall notations in Section
4.3
. Let
\mathcal{A}
be an ergodic automorphism of
\mathcal{X}
and let
d\mathcal{A}
be its linear part. If a subspace
E\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
is both
\Gamma
-rational and invariant under
d\mathcal{A}
, then
d\mathcal{A}\big|_{E}
preserves the lattice
\Lambda=E\cap\Gamma\cong\mathbb{Z}^{\dim E}
and so is represented by a matrix

\displaystyle M_{\mathcal{A},E}\in GL(\dim E,\mathbb{Z})

(see Section
4.3.1
). In particular, since
M_{\mathcal{A},E}
is an integer matrix acting on the lattice
\Lambda
, any Diophantine condition on the pair
(E,d\mathcal{A}|_{E})
is equivalent to a Diophantine condition on subspaces of
\mathbb{R}^{\dim E}
under the action of this integer matrix. Therefore, in this part from (
1
) to (
3
) we collect results on Diophantine subspaces of integer matrices, which will be used later.

1
.

Let
E
be a rational subspace of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
. We say that a subspace
V\subset E
is a
Diophantine subspace of
E
if for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{m}\bigcap E
and any basis
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}\}
of
V
, we have

(9.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V}|z\cdot v_{i}|\geq C_{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V}}\lVert z\rVert^{-\dim E},

where
\lVert\cdot\rVert
is induced by the Euclidean distance.

It is clear that the definition of Diophantine subspace is not dependent on the choice of an integer basis of
E
.

2
.

Suppose
M\in GL(m,\mathbb{Z})
. Let
\mathfrak{q}
be the characteristic polynomial of
M
, and let
\mathfrak{q}=\prod_{i=1}^{l_{0}}\mathfrak{q}_{i}^{c_{i}}

be its prime decomposition over
\mathbb{Q}
. Then we have the corresponding splitting

(9.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}^{m}=\oplus_{i=1}^{l_{0}}\mathcal{F}_{i}

into rational
M
-invariant subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{i}(M)=\ker\mathfrak{q}_{i}^{c_{i}}(M)
.

On each
\mathcal{F}_{i}
,
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
, let
\chi_{i,1}<\cdots<\chi_{i,L(i)}
be the Lyapunov exponents of
M|_{\mathcal{F}_{i}}
and let

(9.3)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{i}=\mathcal{L}_{i,1}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathcal{L}_{i,L(i)}

be the corresponding Lyapunov subspace decomposition. Let

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\max,M}=\oplus_{i=1}^{l_{0}}\mathcal{L}_{i,L(i)}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{L}_{\min,M}=\oplus_{i=1}^{l_{0}}\mathcal{L}_{i,1}.

3
.

We have a decomposition of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
into unstable, neutral, and stable subspaces, denote by
W_{+}(M)
,
W_{0}(M)
, and
W_{-}(M)
, respectively:

\mathbb{R}^{m}\;=\;W_{+}(M)\oplus W_{0}(M)\oplus W_{-}(M),

where

\displaystyle W_{+}(M)=\oplus_{\chi_{i,j}>0}

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{i,j},\quad W_{0}(M)=\oplus_{\chi_{i,j}=0}\mathcal{L}_{i,j},

(9.4)

\displaystyle W_{-}(M)=\oplus_{\chi_{i,j}<0}\mathcal{L}_{i,j}.

Let

\displaystyle\chi(M)=\min\{|\chi_{i,j}|:\,|\chi_{i,j}|\neq 0\}.

Then for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have

\displaystyle\|M^{-\lvert n\rvert}v\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert n\rvert}\|v\|,\qquad v\in W_{+},

\displaystyle\quad\|M^{\lvert n\rvert}v\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert n\rvert}\|v\|,\qquad v\in W_{-},

\displaystyle\|M^{n}v\|

\displaystyle\leq C\,(1+|n|)^{m}\,\|v\|,\qquad v\in W_{0}.

Lemma 9.1
.

Suppose
M\in GL(m,\mathbb{Z})
and
M
is ergodic. Then:

(1)

the following subspaces are Diophantine subspaces of
\mathbb{R}^{m}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\max,M},\quad\mathcal{L}_{\max,M},\quad W_{+}(M),\quad W_{-}(M);

(2)

for any
1\leq i\leq l
and
1\leq j\leq L(i)
,
\mathcal{L}_{i,j}(M)
is a Diophantine subspace of
\mathcal{F}_{i}(M)
.

The proof is left for Appendix
F
.

4
.

Suppose
V
is a subspace of
\mathfrak{n}
and
f
is a function on
\mathcal{X}
. Then for any
v_{i}\in V
,
1\leq i\leq m
and any
z\in\mathbb{Z}
we have

\displaystyle v_{1}\cdots v_{m}\big(f_{i}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z})\big)

\displaystyle=(\Pi_{i=1}^{m}\big\|d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\big\|)\,(w_{1}\cdots w_{m}f)\circ(\mathcal{A}^{z})

where

\displaystyle w_{i}=\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})}{\big\|d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\big\|},\qquad 1\leq i\leq m.

This factorization allows us to extract the norm factors
\big\|d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\big\|
from the multiple partial derivatives.

5
.

Let
\chi_{1}<\cdots<\chi_{l}
be the Lyapunov exponents of
\mathcal{A}
and let

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}=\mathds{L}_{\chi_{1}}\oplus\cdots\oplus\mathds{L}_{\chi_{l}}

be the corresponding Lyapunov subspace decomposition. Then

\displaystyle[\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i}},\,\mathds{L}_{\chi_{j}}]

\displaystyle\subseteq\begin{cases}\displaystyle\{0\},&\text{if }\chi_{i}+\chi_{j}\text{ is not a Lyapunov exponent},\\[2.84526pt]
\mathds{L}_{\chi_{m}},&\text{if }\chi_{i}+\chi_{j}=\chi_{m}.\end{cases}

6
.

Since
V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
is isomorphic to
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
(see Section
8.1
), the restriction
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}|_{V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}}:\,V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}\to\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
in an isomorphism.
Consequently, the rational structure on
V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
induces a rational stricture on
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
. Hence
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}
is represented by an inter matrix
M\in GL(n_{1},\mathbb{Z})
(
n_{1}
is defined in (
1
) of Section
8.1
). Thus, we can assume the notations and constructions in (
2
), i.e., we can define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{i,j}(d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}),\quad\mathcal{F}_{i}(d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}),\quad 1\leq i\leq l_{0},\,\,1\leq j\leq L(i)

correspondingly. We recall that the Lyapunov exponents of
M|_{\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}
is
\chi_{i,j}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,j}})=\mathcal{L}_{i,j},\qquad 1\leq i\leq l_{0},\,\,1\leq j\leq L(i).

7
.

Since the restriction of
\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}|_{V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}}
in an isomorphism, there are
unique subspaces
\mathcal{L}_{i,j}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}
,
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
and
1\leq j\leq L(i)
, such that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{i,j}^{\prime})=\mathcal{L}_{i,j},\quad\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime})=\mathcal{F}_{i},\qquad 1\leq i\leq l_{0},\,\,1\leq j\leq L(i).

We fix a basis

\displaystyle Q_{i,j}^{1},Q_{i,j}^{2},\cdots,Q_{i,j}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}\quad\text{of}\quad\mathcal{L}_{i,j}^{\prime}

(we note that
\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}^{\prime}=\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}
).

Since

\displaystyle\{\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(Q_{i,j}^{1}),\,\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(Q_{i,j}^{2}),\cdots,\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(Q_{i,j}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}})\}\subset\mathcal{L}_{i,j}=\mathfrak{p}_{\mathfrak{n}_{1}}(\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,j}}),

there exist
\eta_{i,j}^{p}\in[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
and
X_{i,j}^{p}\in\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,j}}
such that

(9.5)

\displaystyle Q_{i,j}^{p}+\eta_{i,j}^{p}=X_{i,j}^{p},\qquad 1\leq p\leq\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}.

Let

\displaystyle V_{i,j}\;=\;\mathrm{span}\{X_{i,j}^{p}:1\leq p\leq\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}\}.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an ergodic automorphism of
\mathcal{X}
. Then:

(1)

None of the eigenvalues of
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}
is a root of unity.

(2)

The subspaces
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}
,
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
are
\Gamma
-rational, and for each
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
and
1\leq j\leq L(i)
,
V_{i,j}
is a Diophantine subspace for
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}\subset V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
of type
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
(see (
2
) of Section
8.1
).

The proof is left for Appendix
G
.

8
.

Let
E_{i}=[\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime},\,\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}]\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k}
,
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
. Since each
E_{i}
is independent of the choice of the lift
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
of
\mathcal{F}_{i}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
, one may safely write
E_{i}=[\mathcal{F}_{i},\,\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}]
. Set
E_{[0]}=\{0\}
and

\displaystyle E_{[i]}=E_{1}+E_{2}+\cdots+E_{i},\qquad 1\leq i\leq l_{0}-1.

Lemma 9.3
.

For any
f\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
, we can write

\displaystyle f_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\bot}=\sum_{i=0}^{l_{0}-1}(f_{E_{[i]},o})_{E_{i+1},\bot}

where

\displaystyle f_{E_{[i]},o}-f_{E_{[i+1]},o}=(f_{E_{[i]},o})_{E_{i+1},\bot},\qquad 0\leq i\leq l_{0}-1.

The proof is left for Appendix
H
.

Remark 9.4
.

applies to general case.

9.2.
Basic results

Proposition 9.5
.

Suppose
E\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k}
(see Section
4.3
) is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace and is
d\mathcal{A}
-invariant. For any
z\in\mathbb{Z}
, let

\displaystyle V_{z,E}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\displaystyle W_{-}(M_{\mathcal{A},E}),&\text{if }z\geq 0,\\[2.84526pt]
W_{+}(M_{\mathcal{A},E}),&\text{if }z<0;\end{cases}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\quad V_{1,z,E}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\displaystyle W_{+}(M_{\mathcal{A},E})\oplus W_{0}(M_{\mathcal{A},E}),&\text{if }z\geq 0,\\[2.84526pt]
W_{-}(M_{\mathcal{A},E})\oplus W_{0}(M_{\mathcal{A},E}),&\text{if }z<0.\end{cases}

If none of the eigenvalues of
d\mathcal{A}|_{E}
is a root of unity. Then for any

\displaystyle f_{1}\in W^{\infty,\exp(V_{z,E})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))\quad\text{and}\quad f_{2}\in W^{\infty,\exp(V_{1,z,E})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})),

the followings hold:

(1)

for any basis
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V_{z,E}}\}
of
V_{z,E}
, we can write

\displaystyle(f_{2})_{E,\bot}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}g_{i},

where
g_{i}\in L_{E,\bot}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim V_{z,E}
, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
the equations

\displaystyle v_{i}^{n}\varphi_{i,n}=g_{i}

has a solution
\varphi_{i,n}\in L_{E,\bot}
with the estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{i,n}\rVert\leq C_{n}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z,E}),\,n\dim\mathfrak{n}}.

(2)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{n}e^{-(\chi(M_{\mathcal{A},E})-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert n}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z,E}),n}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z,E}),\,n\dim E}

Proof.

(
1
): From definition, we have

\displaystyle E=V_{z,E}\oplus V_{1,z,E}.

By assumption,
M_{\mathcal{A},E}
is an ergodic automorphism. It follows from Lemma
9.1
that
V_{z,E}
is a Diophantine subspace of
E
(see Section
4.3.2
). Then (
1
) follows directly from
Theorem
7.1
.

(
2
): We fix a basis
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim V_{z,E}}\}
of
V_{z,E}
. Then by using (
1
) for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}

\displaystyle\cdot(f_{2})_{E,\bot}d\varrho\Big|=\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot v_{i}^{n}\varphi_{i,n}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}v_{i}^{n}(f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z})\cdot\varphi_{i,n}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{1})}{=}\Big|\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\rVert^{n}\int_{\mathcal{X}}(w_{z,i}^{n}f_{1})\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot\varphi_{i,n}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\rVert^{n}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}(w_{z,i}^{n}f_{1})\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot\varphi_{i,n}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{2})}{\leq}\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\rVert^{n}\|(w_{z,i}^{n}f_{1})\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\|\,\|\varphi_{i,n}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{3})}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\rVert^{n}\|w_{z,i}^{n}f_{1}\|\,\|\varphi_{i,n}\|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{4})}{\leq}\sum_{i=1}^{\dim V_{z,E}}C_{n,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert n}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z,E}),n}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z,E}),\,n\dim E}

(9.6)

\displaystyle\leq C_{n,\epsilon,1}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert n}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z,E}),n}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z,E}),\,n\dim E}.

Here in
(x_{1})

\displaystyle w_{z,i}=\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})}{\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\rVert},\qquad 1\leq i\leq\dim V_{z,E};

in
(x_{2})
we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality; in
(x_{3})
we recall that
\mathcal{A}^{z}
preserves
\varrho
; in
(x_{4})
we use the estimates in (
3
) of Section
9.1
to estimate
\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(v_{i})\rVert^{n}
.

Then we complete the proof.
∎

Corollary 9.6
.

Suppose
E\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k}
is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace and is
d\mathcal{A}
-invariant.
We can assume the notations and constructions in (
2
) of Section
9.1
, i.e., we can define

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{i,j}(M_{\mathcal{A},E}),\quad\mathcal{F}_{i}(M_{\mathcal{A},E}),\quad 1\leq i\leq l_{0},\,\,1\leq j\leq L(i)

correspondingly.

Choose
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
and
1\leq j\leq L(i)
such that
\mathcal{L}_{i,j}\subset V_{z,E}
(see Proposition
9.5
).
Then for any

\displaystyle f_{1}\in W^{\infty,\exp(\mathcal{L}_{i,j})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))\quad\text{and}\quad f_{2}\in W^{\infty,\exp(E)}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})),

the followings hold:

(1)

for any basis
\{v_{1},\cdots,v_{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}\}
of
\mathcal{L}_{i,j}
, we can write

\displaystyle(f_{2})_{\mathcal{F}_{i},\bot}=\sum_{i=1}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}g_{i},

where
g_{i}\in L_{\mathcal{F}_{i},\bot}
,
1\leq i\leq\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}
, such that for any
n\in\mathbb{N}
the equations

\displaystyle v_{i}^{n}\varphi_{i,n}=g_{i}

has a solution
\varphi_{i,n}\in L_{\mathcal{F}_{i},\bot}
with the estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{i,n}\rVert\leq C_{n}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(E),n\dim\mathfrak{n}}.

(2)

For any
n\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathcal{F}_{i},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{n}e^{-(\chi_{i,j}-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert n}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(\mathcal{L}_{i,j}),n}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{n\dim E}.

Proof.

(
1
): It follows from Lemma
9.1
that
\mathcal{L}_{i,j}
is a Diophantine subspace of
\mathcal{F}_{i}
. Then (
1
) follows directly from
Theorem
7.1
.

(
2
): The proof is exactly the same as that of (
2
) of Proposition
9.5
, except the following changes in (
9.2
):

(1)

change
V_{z,E}
to
\mathcal{L}_{i,j}
;

(2)

in
(x_{4})
, changing
\chi
to
\chi_{i,j}
;

(3)

change
\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z,E}),\,n\dim E}
to
\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{n\dim E}
.

∎

Proposition 9.7
.

Choose
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
and
1\leq j\leq L(i)
such that
\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,j}}\subset V_{z}
(see (
2
) of Section
2.2
). Let

\displaystyle E_{i}=[\mathcal{F}_{i},\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}],\quad V_{1}=\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}\cap\mathds{L}_{-\chi_{i,j}},\quad V_{2}=\mathrm{span}\{V_{1},\,E_{i}\}.

If
\mathfrak{n}_{k}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}
, then
for any

\displaystyle f_{1}\in W^{\infty,\exp(V_{z})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X}))\quad\text{and}\quad f_{2}\in W^{\dim E_{i}+1,\,\exp(V_{2})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})),

the followings hold:

(1)

we can write

\displaystyle(f_{2})_{E_{i},\bot}=\sum_{r=1}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}g_{r},

where
g_{r}\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
,
1\leq r\leq\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}
, such that for any
0<a<\frac{1}{2}
the equation

\displaystyle|X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}(\varphi_{r})=g_{r}

has a solution
\varphi_{r}\in L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
with the estimate

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{r}\rVert\leq C_{a}\,\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}.

(2)

For any
0<a<\frac{1}{2}
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E_{i},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{a}e^{-a|\chi_{i,j}||z|}(1+|z|)^{\dim\mathfrak{n}}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,a}\,\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}.

Remark 9.8
.

explain more about
V_{1}
.

Proof.

(
1
): We will apply Theorem
8.2
. To do so, we first verify its four hypotheses:

(1)

From (
2
) of Lemma
9.2
, we see that
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}\subset V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
is a
\Gamma
-rational subspace;

(2)

V_{i,j}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}
is a Diophantine subspace for
\mathcal{F}_{i}^{\prime}\subset V_{\mathcal{E}^{1}}
of type
\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
(see (
2
) of Lemma
9.2
).
\{X_{i,j}^{p}:1\leq p\leq m(i,j)\}
is a basis of
V_{i,j}
(see (
7
) of Section
9.1
);

(3)

(
5
) of Section
9.1
and the assumption
\mathfrak{n}_{k}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}
show that
V_{1}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}_{k-1}
is dual to
V_{i,j}
(recall (
3
) of Section
8.1
);

(4)

(
5
) of Section
9.1
and the assumption
\mathfrak{n}_{k}\subseteq\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}
show that
V_{2}
is a subalgebra
generated by
V_{1}
and
E_{i}
.

Hence all the hypotheses of Theorem
8.2
are satisfied. Consequently, (
1
) follows from Theorem
8.2
.

(
2
): By using (
1
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}

\displaystyle\cdot(f_{2})_{E_{i},\bot}d\varrho\Big|=\Big|\sum_{r=1}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot|X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}\varphi_{r}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{1})}{=}\Big|\sum_{r=1}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}\int_{\mathcal{X}}|X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}(f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z})\cdot\varphi_{r}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{2})}{\leq}\sum_{r=1}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}\big\||X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}(f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z})\big\|\,\|\varphi_{r}\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=1}^{\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}}C_{a}\big\||X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}(f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z})\big\|\,\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}.

Here in
(x_{1})
we use (
1
) of Lemma
4.3
; in
(x_{2})
we use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

Since
\mathcal{A}
preserves the measure
\varrho
on
\mathcal{X}
,
\pi:\,z\to f\circ\mathcal{A}^{-z}
is a unitary representation of
\mathbb{Z}
on
L^{2}(\mathcal{X})
. By (
3
) of Lemma
4.3
, for any
1\leq r\leq\dim\mathcal{L}_{i,j}
we have

\displaystyle\big\||X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}(f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z})\big\|

\displaystyle=\big\||X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}\pi(-z)f_{1}\big\|=\Big\|\pi(-z)\big(\pi(z)|X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}\pi(-z)\big)f_{1}\Big\|

\displaystyle=\Big\|\big(\pi(z)|X_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}\pi(-z)\big)f_{1}\Big\|=\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(X_{i,j}^{r})\rVert^{a}\,\big\||w_{i,j}^{r}|^{a}f_{1}\big\|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{1})}{\leq}C_{a}e^{-a|\chi_{i,j}||z|}(1+|z|)^{\dim\mathfrak{n}}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),a}

where
w_{x,j}^{r}=\frac{d\mathcal{A}^{z}(X_{i,j}^{r})}{\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(X_{i,j}^{r}\rVert}
. Here in
(x_{1})
we use estimates in (
3
) of Section
9.1
to estimate
\lVert d\mathcal{A}^{z}(X_{i,j}^{r})\rVert
and recall that

V_{i,j}\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{\chi_{i,j}}\subset V_{z}
.

The above discussion shows that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E_{i},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{r=1}^{m(i,j)}C_{a}\big(C_{a}e^{-a|\chi_{i,j}||z|}(1+|z|)^{\dim\mathfrak{n}}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),a}\big)\,\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{a,1}e^{-a|\chi_{i,j}||z|}(1+|z|)^{\dim\mathfrak{n}}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),a}\,\lVert f\rVert_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}.

Hence, we complete the proof.
∎

9.3.
Proof of Theorem
2.1

Throughout, fix

f_{1}\in W^{\infty,\exp(V_{z})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})),\quad f_{2}\in W^{\infty,\exp(V_{1,z})}(L^{2}(\mathcal{X})).

We argue by induction on the nilpotency step
k
of
N
.

Base Case
k=1
:
If
N
has step
1
, then
N\cong\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathcal{X}=N/\Gamma
is a torus. Let
E=\mathfrak{n}
. Then
\mathbb{T}_{E}=\mathcal{X}
and

\displaystyle(f_{2})_{E,o}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{X}}\pi(t)f_{2}(x)d\varrho(t)=\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}(x)d\varrho(x),\qquad\text{and}

(9.7)

\displaystyle(f_{2})_{E,\bot}

\displaystyle=f_{2}-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}(x)d\varrho(x)

(see (
1
) of Section
7.1
).

Clearly,
E
is both
\Gamma
-rational and
d\mathcal{A}
-invariant. By (
1
) of Lemma
9.2
,
d\mathcal{A}
is an ergodic automorphism. In this case,
V_{z}=V_{z,E}
(see Proposition
9.5
). It follows from (
2
) of Proposition

9.5
that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}

\displaystyle\cdot(f_{2})_{E,\bot}d\varrho\Big|\leq C_{m}e^{-\chi\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),m\dim\mathfrak{n}}.

This gives that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot f_{2}d\varrho-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\cdot\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{1})}{=}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot f_{2}d\varrho-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,o}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z,1}),m\dim\mathfrak{n}}.

Here in
(x_{1})
, by using (
9.3
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,o}d\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}d\varrho\cdot\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\cdot\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho.

Thus the result holds when
k=1
.

Inductive Step:
Suppose the result holds for all simply-connected nilpotent groups of step
\leq k-1
, and now let
N
have step
k\geq 2
. We first establish the result under two special cases.

Lemma 9.9
.

Under the inductive hypothesis, the desired decay estimate holds for step
k
simply-connected nilpotent groups in either of the following two cases:

(1)

d\mathcal{A}\big|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
has only roots of unity;

(2)

d\mathcal{A}\big|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
has no roots of unity.

Proof.

Case 1:
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
only has roots of unity.

We recall notations in Section
9.1
. By Lemma
9.3
,
we can write

(9.8)

\displaystyle(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\bot}=\sum_{i=1}^{l_{0}}((f_{2})_{E_{[i-1]},o})_{E_{i},\bot}.

We note that for any
z\in\mathbb{Z}
and any
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
, either
\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,1}}\subset V_{z}
or
\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,L(i)}}\subset V_{z}
. Let

\displaystyle j_{i}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\displaystyle 1,&\text{if }\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,1}}\subset V_{z},\\[2.84526pt]
L(i),&\text{if }\mathds{L}_{\chi_{i,L(i)}}\subset V_{z}.\end{cases}

For
i
and corresponding
j_{i}
it follows from (
2
) of Proposition
9.7
that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot((f_{2})_{E_{[i-1]},o})_{E_{i},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2})|\chi_{i,j_{i}}||z|}(1+|z|)^{\dim\mathfrak{n}}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot\big\|(f_{2})_{E_{[i-1]},o}\big\|_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}

\displaystyle\overset{(x_{1})}{\leq}C_{\epsilon,1}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)|\chi_{i,j_{i}}||z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{2}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,1}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)|\chi_{i,j_{i}}||z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim E_{i}+1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,1}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}\,.

Here in
(x_{1})
we use (
4a
) of Lemma
7.2
.

This together with (
9.8
) gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{l_{0}}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot((f_{2})_{E_{[i-1]},o})_{E_{i},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

(9.9)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}.

Case 2:
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
has no roots of unity.

It follows from (
2
) of
Proposition
9.5
(where we let
E=\mathfrak{n}_{k}
) that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi(M_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}_{k}})-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}),m\dim\mathfrak{n}_{k}}

(9.10)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),m\dim\mathfrak{n}}.

Conclusion.

We note that if
\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}=\{0\}
, then
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
has no roots of unity. Then it follows from (
9.3
) and (
9.3
) that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

(9.11)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\,\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}.

Since

\displaystyle f_{2}=(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\,\bot}+(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\,o},

it remains to estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho.

It follows from (
6
) of Lemma
7.2
that

(9.12)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\,o}d\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\circ(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}})^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},\,o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}.

We recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}:=(N/\exp(\mathfrak{n}_{k}))\bigl/\bigl(\Gamma/(\exp(\mathfrak{n}_{k})\cap\Gamma)\bigr)

(see (
2
) of Section
7
). It is clear that
N/\exp(\mathfrak{n}_{k})
is simply connected of step
k-1
.

\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}
is an ergodic automorphism of
\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
. Thus, we can define
\rho(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})
,
V_{z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})
,
V_{1,z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})
and
\chi(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})
for
\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}
correspondingly.

It follows from inductive assumption that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\circ(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\big(\|(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\|_{\exp(V_{z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}})),m}

\displaystyle\cdot\lVert(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}})),\,m(\dim\mathfrak{n}-\dim\mathfrak{n}_{k})}\big)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})|z|}\big(\lVert(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})),\,\frac{1}{2}}

(9.13)

\displaystyle\cdot\big\|(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})),\,(\dim\mathfrak{n}-\dim\mathfrak{n}_{k})+1}\big).

We use
\mathfrak{i}
to denote the projection from
\mathfrak{n}
to
\mathfrak{n}/\mathfrak{n}_{k}
. We note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{i}(V_{z})=V_{z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{i}(V_{1,z})=V_{1,z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}).

It follows from (
4b
) of Lemma
7.2
we have

\displaystyle\|(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\|_{\exp(V_{z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}})),s}

\displaystyle\leq\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),s}\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\lVert(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})),s}

\displaystyle\leq\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,s}

for any
s\geq 0
.

We also note that

\displaystyle\chi\leq\chi(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})\quad\text{and}\quad\rho\leq\rho(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}).

Further, from (
7
) of Lemma
7.2
we have

(9.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho.

Consequently, the above discussion shows that (
9.3
) can be simplified as

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}\circ(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}})^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,m(\dim\mathfrak{n}-\dim\mathfrak{n}_{k})}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z},\,\frac{1}{2})}\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,(\dim\mathfrak{n}-\dim\mathfrak{n}_{k})+1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

(9.15)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z},\,\frac{1}{2})}\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}.

Finally, it follows from (
9.3
), (
9.12
) and (
9.3
) that: for any
m\in\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot f_{2}d\varrho-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,o}d\varrho-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z},\,\frac{1}{2})}\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}.

∎

We now complete the inductive step. Clearly, if
\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}=\{0\}
, the inductive step is completed by Lemma
9.9
. Now assume
\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}\neq\{0\}
.
We have a decomposition of
dA|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}_{k}=\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}\oplus\mathfrak{n}_{k,2},

where
\mathfrak{n}_{k,i}=\mathfrak{n}_{k}\cap\mathfrak{n}^{(i)}
,
i=1,\,2
. Then both
\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}
and
\mathfrak{n}_{k,2}
are
\Gamma
-rational
d\mathcal{A}
-invariant subspaces.

If
\mathfrak{n}_{k,2}=0
, then
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
has no roots of unity. If
\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}=0
, then
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}}
only has no roots of unity. For either case, Lemma
9.9
applies. Now we suppose
\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}\neq 0
and
\mathfrak{n}_{k,2}\neq 0
.

It follows from (
2
) of
Proposition
9.5
(where we let
E=\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}
) that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi(M_{\mathcal{A},\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}})-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z,\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z,\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}),m\dim\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}

(9.16)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),m\dim\mathfrak{n}}.

It remains to estimate

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},o}d\varrho.

Similar to (
9.12
), we have

(9.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},\,o}d\varrho=\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},o}\circ(\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}})^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},\,o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}.

We recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}:=(N/\exp(\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}))\bigl/\bigl(\Gamma/(\exp(\mathfrak{n}_{k,1})\cap\Gamma)\bigr)

It is clear that
N_{1}=N/\exp(\mathfrak{n}_{k,1})
is simply connected. Since
\mathfrak{n}_{k,2}\neq 0
,
N_{1}
remains a
k
-step nilpotent group. We denote by
\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{\prime}
the
k
-th element of the descending central series of
\text{Lie}(N_{1})=\mathfrak{n}/\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}
. Then
d(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}})|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{\prime}}
is isomorphic to
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,2}}
. Since
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,2}}
only has roots of unity,
(
1
) of Lemma
9.9
applies
and gives, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},o}\circ(\mathcal{A}|_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}})^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}}(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

(9.18)

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z},\,\frac{1}{2})}\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}.

Similar (
9.14
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}}(f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}\int_{\mathcal{X}_{\mathfrak{n}_{k,1}}}(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k},o}d\varrho_{1,\mathfrak{n}_{k}}=\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho.

This together with (
9.3
), (
9.17
), (
9.3
) give

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot f_{2}d\varrho-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,\bot}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}\circ\mathcal{A}^{z}\cdot(f_{2})_{E,o}d\varrho-\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{1}d\varrho\int_{\mathcal{X}}f_{2}d\varrho\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z},\,\frac{1}{2})}\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}

for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
. This completes the inductive step. Hence, we complete the proof.

9.4.
Proof of (
2
) Theorem
2.2

We prove by induction on
n
.

Base Case
n=2
:
For any
f_{1},\,f_{2}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})
and any
z_{1},\,z_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}
, it follows from
Theorem
2.1
that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ z_{1},\,f_{2}\circ z_{2})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\mathfrak{m}(f_{2})\big|

\displaystyle=\big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ(z_{1}-z_{2}),\,f_{2})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\mathfrak{m}(f_{2})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z_{1}-z_{2}\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{1}-z_{2}}),m}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z_{1}-z_{2}|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\leq}C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z_{1}-z_{2}\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z_{1}-z_{2}|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big\|f_{2}\big\|_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
6.1
) of Section
6.1
.

Thus the result holds when
n=2
.

Inductive Step:
Suppose the argument holds for
n=l
; that is, for

z_{1},\dots,z_{l}\in\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{and}\quad f_{1},\dots,f_{l}\in C^{\bar{r}}(\mathcal{X}),

we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ z_{1},\dots,f_{l}\circ z_{l})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{l})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)(\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq l}\lvert z_{i}-z_{j}\rvert)m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

(9.19)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq l}\lvert z_{i}-z_{j}\rvert)}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big).

Suppose

\displaystyle z_{1},\cdots,z_{l+1}\in\mathbb{Z}\quad\text{ and }\quad f_{1},\cdots,f_{l+1}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{X}).

Without loss of generality, assume

\displaystyle z_{l+1}=\min_{1\leq j\leq l+1}z_{j}.

Then we have

(9.20)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},\cdots,f_{l+1}\circ z_{l+1}\big)=\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}\circ z,f_{l+1}),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\big(f_{1}\circ(z_{1}-z_{l+1}-z)\big)\big(f_{2}\circ(z_{2}-z_{l+1}-z)\big)\cdots\big(f_{l}\circ(z_{l}-z_{l+1}-z)\big)

and

\displaystyle z=\min_{1\leq i\leq l}(z_{i}-z_{l+1})>0.

It follows from Theorem
2.1
that for any
m\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}\circ z,f_{l+1})-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}\lVert f_{l+1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

(9.21)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{l+1}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}.

Since
z\geq 0
,
V_{z}=W_{-}
, i.e., the stable subspace with respect to
\mathcal{A}
. We note that
z_{i}-z_{l+1}-z\geq 0
,
1\leq i\leq l
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|d\mathcal{A}^{z_{i}-z_{l+1}-z}|_{V_{z}}\big\|\leq Ce^{-\frac{1}{2}\chi(z_{i}-z_{l+1}-z)}\leq C,\qquad 1\leq i\leq l.

This together with (
4
) of Section
9.1
gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),m}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),C^{m}}\leq C_{m,l}\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m}},\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z}),C^{1}}\leq C_{l}\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{1}}.

Let

\displaystyle z_{0}=\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq l+1}\lvert z_{i}-z_{j}\rvert.

The above discussions with (
9.20
) and (
9.4
) give

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},\cdots,f_{l+1}\circ z_{l+1}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m}}\big)\lVert f_{l+1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{1}}\big)\big\|f_{l+1}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z\rvert m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z|}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)z_{0}m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

(9.22)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho z_{0}}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big).

We note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},\cdots,f_{l}\circ z_{l}\big).

Then for any
m\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})\big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},\cdots,f_{l}\circ z_{l}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{l})\Big|\cdot|\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})|

\displaystyle\overset{(1)}{\leq}\Big(C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)(\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq l}\lvert z_{i}-z_{j}\rvert)m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq l}\lvert z_{i}-z_{j}\rvert)}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big)\Big)\cdot|\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)(\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq l}\lvert z_{i}-z_{j}\rvert)m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(\min_{1\leq i\neq j\leq l}\lvert z_{i}-z_{j}\rvert)}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)z_{0}m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

(9.23)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho z_{0}}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big)

Here in
(1)
we use the inductive assumption (
9.4
).

Consequently, for any
m\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ z_{1},\dots,f_{l}\circ z_{l+1})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\Big|\mathfrak{m}(f_{1}\circ z_{1},\dots,f_{l}\circ z_{l+1})-\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})\Big|

\displaystyle+\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F})\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{l+1})\big|

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{m,\epsilon,l}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)z_{0}m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,l}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho z_{0}}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{l+1}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big).

Here in
(1)
we use (
9.4
) and (
9.4
).

This completes the inductive step. Hence, we complete the proof.

9.5.
Proof of (
1
) Theorem
2.2

Suppose

\displaystyle z_{1},\,z_{2},\,z_{3}\in\mathbb{Z},\quad f_{1},\,f_{2},\,f_{3}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathfrak{m}(f_{i})=0,\,\,1\leq i\leq 3.

Without loss of generality, assume that
z_{1}\leq z_{2}\leq z_{3}
. Then

\displaystyle z_{3}-z_{1}=\max_{1\leq i\neq j\leq 3}|z_{i}-z_{j}|

Case I
: Suppose
z_{3}-z_{2}>\frac{1}{2}(z_{3}-z_{1})
.

We have

(9.24)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},f_{3}\circ z_{3}\big)=\mathfrak{m}\big(\mathcal{F}\circ(z_{2}-z_{3}),f_{3}\big).

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}=\big(f_{1}\circ(z_{1}-z_{2})\big)f_{2}.

It follows from Theorem
2.1
and the fact that
\mathfrak{m}(f_{3})=0
that for any
m\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}(\mathcal{F}\circ(z_{2}-z_{3}),f_{3})\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\epsilon)\lvert z_{2}-z_{3}\rvert m}\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),m}\lVert f_{3}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z_{2}-z_{3}}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho|z_{2}-z_{3}|}\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{3}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z_{2}-z_{3}}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\chi-\epsilon)(z_{3}-z_{1})m}\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),m}\lVert f_{3}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}

(9.25)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(z_{3}-z_{1})}\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{3}\big\|_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}.

Here in
(1)
we recall the assumption
z_{3}-z_{2}>\frac{1}{2}(z_{3}-z_{1})
.

Since
z_{2}-z_{3}\leq 0
,
V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}=W_{+}
, i.e., the unstable subspace with respect to
\mathcal{A}
. We note that
z_{1}-z_{2}\leq 0
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|d\mathcal{A}^{z_{1}-z_{2}}|_{V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}}\big\|\leq Ce^{-\frac{1}{2}\chi(z_{2}-z_{1})}\leq C.

This together with (
4
) of Section
9.1
gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),m}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),C^{m}}\leq C_{m}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{m}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{m}},\qquad\text{and}

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),C^{1}}\leq C\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{1}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{1}}.

The above discussions with (
9.24
) and (
9.5
) give

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},f_{3}\circ z_{3}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\chi-\epsilon)(z_{3}-z_{1})m}\|\mathcal{F}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),m}\lVert f_{3}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(z_{3}-z_{1})}\lVert\mathcal{F}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z_{2}-z_{3}}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\big\|f_{3}\big\|_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\chi-\epsilon)(z_{3}-z_{1})m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

(9.26)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(z_{3}-z_{1})}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\lVert f_{i}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big).

Hence, we get the result.

Case II
: Suppose
z_{3}-z_{2}<\frac{1}{2}(z_{3}-z_{1})
. Then

\displaystyle z_{2}-z_{1}

\displaystyle=z_{2}-z_{3}-(z_{1}-z_{3})>\frac{1}{2}(z_{1}-z_{3})-(z_{1}-z_{3})

(9.27)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(z_{3}-z_{1})>0.

We rewrite

(9.28)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},f_{3}\circ z_{3}\big)=\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ(z_{1}-z_{2}),\mathcal{G}\big)

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=f_{2}\big(f_{3}\circ(z_{3}-z_{2})\big).

It follows from Theorem
2.1
and the fact that
\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})=0
that for any
m\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ(z_{1}-z_{2}),\mathcal{G}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-(\chi-\frac{\epsilon}{2})\lvert z_{1}-z_{1}\rvert m}\|f_{1}\|_{\exp(V_{z_{1}-z_{2}}),m}\lVert\mathcal{G}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2})\rho|z_{1}-z_{2}|}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{\exp(V_{z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,\frac{1}{2}}\big\|\mathcal{G}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\text{(1)}}{\leq}C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\chi-\frac{\epsilon}{2})(z_{3}-z_{1})m}\|f_{1}\|_{C^{m}}\lVert\mathcal{G}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

(9.29)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2})\rho(z_{3}-z_{1})}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{1}}\big\|\mathcal{G}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}.

Here in
(1)
we use (
9.5
).

Since
z_{1}-z_{2}\leq 0
,
V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}=W_{+}\oplus W_{0}
, i.e., the weak unstable subspace with respect to
\mathcal{A}
. We note that
z_{1}-z_{2}\leq 0
. Then

\displaystyle\big\|d\mathcal{A}^{z_{1}-z_{2}}|_{V_{1,z_{2}-z_{3}}}\big\|\leq C(1+|z_{1}-z_{2}|)^{\dim\mathfrak{n}}.

This together with (
4
) of Section
9.1
give

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{G}\|_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),m}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathcal{G}\|_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),C^{m}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{m}(1+|z_{1}-z_{2}|)^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{m}}\lVert f_{2}\rVert_{C^{m}}

for any
m\geq 0
.

The above discussions with (
9.28
) and (
9.5
) give

\displaystyle\Big|\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},f_{3}\circ z_{3}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\chi-\frac{\epsilon}{2})(z_{3}-z_{1})m}\|f_{1}\|_{C^{m}}\lVert\mathcal{G}\rVert_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,m\dim\mathfrak{n}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2})\rho(z_{3}-z_{1})}\lVert f_{1}\rVert_{C^{1}}\big\|\mathcal{G}\big\|_{\exp(V_{1,z_{1}-z_{2}}),\,\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\chi-\frac{\epsilon}{2})(z_{3}-z_{1})m}(1+|z_{1}-z_{2}|)^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\epsilon}{2})\rho(z_{3}-z_{1})}(1+|z_{1}-z_{2}|)^{(\dim\mathfrak{n}+1)\dim\mathfrak{n}}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C_{m,\epsilon,2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\chi-\epsilon)(z_{3}-z_{1})m}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}\big)

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,1}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\rho(z_{3}-z_{1})}\big(\Pi_{i=1}^{3}\|f_{i}\|_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}\big).

This together with (
9.5
) give the estimates.
Hence, we complete the proof.

9.6.
Proof of Theorem
2.4

9.6.1.
Notations and basic facts

1
.

We have the Lyapunov subspace decomposition for
d\alpha
:

(9.30)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}=\oplus_{\chi\in\Lambda}\mathcal{W}_{\chi}

such that for any
\chi\in\Lambda
and any
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
,
\mathcal{W}_{\chi}
is a Lyapunov subspace of
d\alpha(z)
with Lyapunov exponent
\chi(z)
. For each
\chi\in\Lambda\backslash\{0\}
,
\ker\chi
is called a Lyapunov hyperplane in
\mathbb{R}^{l}
.

We also have the Lyapunov subspace decomposition for
d\alpha|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}
:

(9.31)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]=\oplus_{\chi\in\Lambda_{1}}\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\chi}

where
\overline{\mathcal{W}}_{\chi}=\mathcal{W}_{\chi}+[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]
. It is clear that
\Lambda_{1}\subseteq\Lambda
.

2
.

For
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
we say that
z
is
regular
if:

(1)

z\notin\bigcup_{\chi\in\Lambda\backslash\{0\}}\ker\chi
for any
\chi\in\Lambda\backslash\{0\}
;

(2)

and
\chi_{1}(z)\neq\chi_{2}(z)
for any
\chi_{1}\neq\chi_{2}\in\Lambda
.

Recall the decomposition (
2.4
) of Section
2.4
. We have the following result, whose proof is left for Appendix
I
:

Lemma 9.10
.

There is a regular
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
such that
\mathfrak{n}^{(z,2)}=\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}
.

3
.

Let
z_{0}
be as described in Lemma
9.10
. Denote by
\mathcal{A}=\alpha(z_{0})
. Since
z_{0}
is regular, the Lyapunov subspace decomposition of
d\mathcal{A}
(resp.
d\mathcal{A}_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}
) coincides with the decomposition (
9.30
) (resp. (
9.31
)).

Lemma 9.11
.

If there is
z\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
such that
d\alpha(z)|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}
is ergodic, then
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}
is ergodic.

Since
d\mathcal{A}
is ergodic, for any
1\leq i\leq l_{0}
,
\chi_{i,1}\neq 0
. Since the Lyapunov subspace decomposition of
d\mathcal{A}
coincides with the decomposition (
9.30
), there is
\chi(i)\in\Lambda\backslash\{0\}
such that
\mathcal{L}_{i,1}(d\mathcal{A})=\mathcal{W}_{\chi(i)}
.

9.6.2.
Proof strategies

Let
\mathcal{A}
be as described in (
3
) of Section
9.6.1
. Moreover, assume that
d\mathcal{A}|_{\mathfrak{n}/[\mathfrak{n},\mathfrak{n}]}
is ergodic.
For any

\mathfrak{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{nl}\setminus\{0\}
, where
z_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}^{l}
,
1\leq i\leq n
define

\displaystyle\Theta(\mathfrak{n},\,d\alpha,\mathfrak{z})=\min_{\chi\in\Lambda\backslash\{0\},\,i\neq j}\Big|\chi(\frac{z_{i}-z_{j}}{\lVert z_{i}-z_{j}\rVert})\Big|

A crucial step toward the proof of Proof of Theorem
2.4
is the following result:

Theorem 9.12
.

For any
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})
and any
z_{1},\cdots,z_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
, if there exists
\mathfrak{r}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Theta(\mathfrak{n},\,d\alpha,\mathfrak{z})\geq\mathfrak{r},\qquad\text{where }\mathfrak{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{nl},

then for any
m\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\Big|

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},\cdots,f_{n}\circ z_{n}\big)-\mathfrak{m}(f_{1})\cdots\mathfrak{m}(f_{n})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,m}e^{-(\mathfrak{r}-\epsilon)\,m\min_{1\leq p\neq j\leq n}\lVert z_{p}-z_{j}\rVert}\,\Pi_{p=1}^{n}\lVert f_{p}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}}

\displaystyle+\delta C_{\epsilon,\mathfrak{r}}e^{-\mathfrak{r}(\frac{1}{2}-\epsilon)\,\min_{1\leq p\neq j\leq n}\lVert z_{p}-z_{j}\rVert}\,\Pi_{p=1}^{n}\lVert f_{p}\rVert_{C^{\dim\mathfrak{n}+1}}.

Proposition 9.13
.

For any
f_{1},\cdots,f_{n}\in C^{\infty}(\mathcal{X})
and any
z_{1},\cdots,z_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}
, if there exists
\mathfrak{r}>0
such that

\displaystyle\Theta(\mathfrak{n},\,d\alpha,\mathfrak{z})\geq\mathfrak{r},\qquad\text{where }\mathfrak{z}=(z_{1},\dots,z_{n})\in\mathbb{Z}^{nl},

then the following hold:

(1)

for any
m\geq 0
we have

\displaystyle\Big|

\displaystyle\mathfrak{m}\big(f_{1}\circ z_{1},f_{2}\circ z_{2},\cdots,f_{n}\circ z_{n}\big)

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{m}\big((f_{1})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{(1)},\,o}\circ z_{1},(f_{2})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{(1)},\,o}\circ z_{2},\cdots,(f_{n})_{\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{(1)},\,o}\circ z_{n}\big)\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C_{\epsilon,m}e^{-(\mathfrak{r}-\epsilon)\,m\min_{1\leq p\neq j\leq n}\lVert z_{p}-z_{j}\rVert}\,\Pi_{p=1}^{n}\lVert f_{p}\rVert_{C^{m\dim\mathfrak{n}}},

where
\mathfrak{n}_{k}^{(1)}=\mathfrak{n}^{(z_{0},1)}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{k}
;

(2)

if
\mathfrak{n}_{k}=\mathfrak{n}^{(2)}\cap\mathfrak{n}_{k}
, then
we have

\displaystyle\Big|
